# Sourcing screen A: Zotero add-ons on Zotero 10 — evidence

- **Date:** 2026-10-03
- **Method:** a desk read per candidate family at a pin, then an adversarial verifier per family that defaulted to refuted. Six families: identity-hygiene, identifiers-metadata, counts-summaries, attachments-fulltext-ocr, notes-ui-workflow, search-new. Agents returned per-must results and exit lines only, never verdicts. Live legs are pending (at 2026-10-03; superseded: they ran on 2026-10-04, as below and in §6.4). On 2026-10-03 a normalisation pass re-judged A3 under the operator's ruled standards (§2a, "Standards as applied"), desk then adversarial verifier; cells it changed carry ‡. On 2026-10-04 a second normalisation pass re-judged A3 under the operator's "judged on what it does by itself" principle (§2a, "Second ruling"), desk then adversarial verifier; cells it changed carry §. Live legs ran on 2026-10-04 (Zotero 10.0.5 test instance with BBT 9.0.68, with snapshot/restore; Obsidian 1.13.7 scratch vault driven through the Obsidian CLI; read-only BBT request replays against production BBT 9.0.63); results carry ◊. Later on 2026-10-04 three verified checks (semantic-zotero, remote-write-routes and scanner-vs-lifecycle), each a check then an adversarial verifier whose result is used, re-read the cells they list; they changed no result, and their per-row lines in §4 begin "¤ 2026-10-04 check" (§7 items 35–43). Paths under /tmp/lane23-live/ are session-local raw logs: they are archived outside the repository, in ~/rv-lanes23-raw-logs-2026-10-04.tar.gz, and are not retained in it. The scanner-vs-lifecycle logs (/tmp/lane23-live/scanner-vs-lifecycle/) postdate that archive and are not in it, and the checks' other scratch reads under /tmp are not archived. A third normalisation on 2026-10-04 re-judged A3 under the operator's trigger-neutral, right-by-construction standard (§2a, "Third ruling"), desk then adversarial verifier per family; cells it changed carry ∆. A fourth reading on 2026-10-04 re-derived A3 under the operator's "beyond commands on the selection" ruling (§2a, "Fourth ruling"), re-derivation then adversarial verifier per family, starting from each row's ∆ line; cells it changed carry ◇.
- **Spec:** 2026-10-03-substrate-components-design.md
- **Binds:** nothing. This is evidence the spec is built from; the spec carries the verdicts.

**Reading guide.** §1 states the question and the musts; §2a ("Standards as applied", with its "Second ruling" of 2026-10-04) holds the standards every §3 A3 result is judged under, and where it differs from §1's A3 wording, §2a applies. §3 holds 54 candidate rows, numbered 1–54; §6.4 adds a supplementary A1 table, the 27 add-ons of the 2026-10-04 A1 batch with the raw log behind each reading. §4 gives each row's pin, write list and the dated line behind every mark. Marks on a §3 cell accumulate, and a later pass keeps the earlier ones: † the family verifier refuted the desk, by changing the result or its basis (§3); ‡ the 2026-10-03 normalisation pass changed the A3 result (§2a); § (with no section number after it) the 2026-10-04 by-itself pass changed the A3 result (§2a, "Second ruling"); ◊ the 2026-10-04 live run set or confirmed the result (§6.4); ¤ a verified check of 2026-10-04 changed the result; ∆ the 2026-10-04 third normalisation changed the A3 result (§2a, "Third ruling"); ◇ the 2026-10-04 fourth reading changed the A3 result (§2a, "Fourth ruling"). The three checks changed none, so no §3 cell carries ¤; their §4 lines begin with ¤. In §4, a line beginning ‡, §, ◊, ¤, ∆ or ◇ records what that pass, reading, run or check found for the row. Results are per must (pass, fail, needs-live or n/a) and are not verdicts: whether a vault declares or rejects an add-on is decided in the spec (Binds, above).

## 1. Question and musts

Question: which Zotero add-ons does a research-vault vault declare, and which does it reject, on Zotero 10?

Production Zotero is 10.0.5 with Better BibTeX 9.0.63 (probe, 2026-10-03). Profile: `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/AppData/Roaming/Zotero/Zotero/Profiles/881hrcxd.default/`. For an installed add-on, A1 is decided by extensions.json: appDisabled=false means Zotero 10.0.5 will load it, and userDisabled is the author's switch, not a load failure. For a non-installed add-on, the repo-manifest and shipped-release-manifest ranges are recorded and A1 is needs-live (at 2026-10-03; superseded for the 27 add-ons of the 2026-10-04 A1 batch, whose A1 is the observed reading in §6.4; rows 16, 20, 35, 43 and 53 stay needs-live). A1 is never decided from a manifest range.

Step map (closed set): vault-setup, capture, compile, search, scoping-review, long-form, daily-log, publish.

- **A1 Loads on Zotero 10** — observed active-capable (appDisabled=false) on the running version, never inferred from a manifest range.
- **A2 Serves or protects a step in the step map.** Name the step(s).
- **A3 Preserves lane 1's seam:** does not destroy another writer's data in Extra, tags, fields or child notes (stripping or rewriting other plugins' lines fails), and does not break citation-key identity (any merge, re-key or item replacement must go through BBT's consolidation).
- **A4 What it writes is known:** list every write target (fields, Extra line grammar, tags, child notes, attachments, state outside the item) with cites. Result is pass only when the write list is complete from code; needs-live when a before/after reading on the test instance is required to close it. Describe the live leg needed.

Results are pass, fail, needs-live or n/a. Basis is "evidence" (shipped code, manifest, schema, config) or "claim" (README or description). Every cite is file:line at the pin named in the row, or a local path. Where this record does not say "claim", the basis is evidence.

- A fresh search for capabilities that §38 and §22–§37 do not cover. Only the search-new family did this (§2.4).

### 2.5 Dropped by the search-new worker (cap 10)

Each line gives what was dropped and why.

- **AgiNetz/semantic-zotero:** its §38 status may have changed. Screened in counts-summaries (row 25). No other §38 dead or capped entry changed in the catalogue.
- **Interface-only (34 rows):** UI only, so no step is served. Zotero Reading List was kept because it writes Extra.
- **AI chat, assistants and translation (about 63 rows):** they serve no step.
- **Reader UI (about 43 rows):** they serve no step.
- **AI note writers (13 rows):** they would do compile inside Zotero as child notes. Hazard: capture would carry those notes.
- **Enrichment and journal metrics** (Green Frog, zotero-if, Jasminum, zotero-inspire, AstroZotero, AlphaPulse, the CCF and ranking family): the same class §38 already records. Hazard: their grammars would need preserving.
- **Shadow-library fetchers** (zotero-scihub, zotero-scipdf, sci-download, LGFetcher): a public repository cannot declare them.
- **Extra PDF-to-Markdown and OCR plugins** (seven): §38's OCR group already covers the class.
- **Direct writers into a Markdown workspace** (zotero-mineru-obsidian, zotero-project-manager, zotero-sidemark): they would write a vault outside the CLI. Hazard noted.
- **AI tag writers** (four): hazard, because they rewrite the tag vocabulary capture carries.
- **Agent and automation bridges** (nine, including zotero-js-bridge, which runs arbitrary JS, and cboulanger/zotero-rag): §24–§27 shape. ZotSeek represents search.
- **dralkh/seerai:** an AI assistant whose systematic-review support is a claim only. A scoping-review near-miss.
- **Moonweave-Research/zotero-reading-flow:** overlaps Reading List and Reading Time. A daily-log near-miss.
- **zotero-reading-heatmap (9.99.99), zotracer (7.0.\*):** daily-log, but no Zotero-10 release.
- **daily-folder-for-zotero:** collection UI, not a log.
- **Juris-M/zotero-odf-scan-plugin:** a long-form near-miss. zotxt represents long-form.
- **eaiser-citation:** writes into documents, not the library.
- **bibtex-clean:** a long-form or publish near-miss with no stars.
- **heimi98/zotero-deduplicator (9.\*):** no Zotero-10 release. Attachment Scanner covers it.
- **Capped at Zotero 6** (ZoteroDuplicatesMerger, zotero-storage-scanner, zotfile, zotero-mdnotes, zotodo, zotero-updateifs): dead.
- **zotero-book-splitter:** narrow. A capture near-miss.
- **ZotAssets:** overlaps Attanger and ZotMoov.
- **Zotero-add-items-from-text, zotero-smart-clipboard-import:** AI class. A capture near-miss.
- **Zotitle, zotero-search-replace:** bulk field rewriters that overlap Linter.
- **zotero-syllabus:** serves no step.
- **External-store syncs** (eight): outside the step map.
- **Settings backup** (tara, zotero-settings-sync): not a step.
- **Feed and discovery tools** (seven): source finding is a vault skill. A search near-miss.
- **Not add-ons** (MetaScreener, prismaid, a browser extension, ZoDAV, awesome lists): out of class.
- **Renames and duplicates** (zotlit, zotero-citation, zotero-reading-flow, fred_zotero): recorded so they are not counted twice.

## 2a. Standards as applied (2026-10-03)

The operator ruled these standards on 2026-10-03. For every A3 result in §3 they replace §1's A3 wording (line 18). Where the ruling is silent, the reading applied is named below. ‡ marks a cell whose result this pass changed; † keeps its §3 meaning.

**Machine surfaces.** The guard's roster is the standard (hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-23; pre-lane-2 decision 11, 2026-09-17-pre-lane-2-design.md:27, makes the guard authoritative). It covers any path whose first component is literature/, log/, wiki/ or fulltext/, anything under system/propagations/, and the root files log.md, inbox/review-queue.md and system/bibliography.json. The rest of system/ (system/templates/, system/bases/, system/glossary.md) is hand-edited and is not a machine surface. README.md:7 names system/ whole; that is a recorded README drift, not the standard. The rulings on B3, C1, B4 and C5, which use this roster, judge Obsidian plugins and decide no cell in this screen.

**A3.** A3 judges two things only:

- (a) the add-on does not destroy or rewrite another writer's data in Extra, tags, fields or child notes; (superseded 2026-10-04 by the by-itself wording under "Second ruling" below)
- (b) the add-on does not change an item's citation key by itself: an automatic re-key, a pinned-key rewrite, or a key-format change it applies unasked. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**Merging is out of A3.** This is a Zotero property, recorded for the spec. On this profile every merge path bypasses Better BibTeX's consolidation, Zotero's own Merge pane included:

- The pane calls mergeItems.mjs directly (duplicatesMergePane.js:34, 203-211 @ ca61760).
- Zotero.Items.merge, the only function BBT patches, is a deprecated shim over the same module (items.js:1037-1041 @ ca61760).
- BBT's shim does nothing while extraMergeCitekeys, extraMergeTeX and extraMergeCSL are false. They ship false and production leaves them unset (defaults.js:54-56; better-bibtex.ts:105 @ f3c9299).
- What every merge leaves is a dc:replaces relation (mergeItems.mjs:426-477 @ ca61760), which lifecycle.py:33-83 reads.

The hazard is therefore Zotero's default behaviour, not a property of any add-on. A merge-capable add-on is judged on (a) and (b) like any other, and writes it makes before it calls the merge are its own (row 4).

**Readings applied where the ruling is silent.** Apart from the first, these are the workers' and verifiers' readings, not rulings.

- **Paths.** (superseded 2026-10-04) This reading is what the ruled wording itself gives. "By itself" qualifies (b) only, and the hand-edit carve-out was ruled for B3/C1 only, so (a) was applied to every shipped path, including commands the person runs. That is why §7 item 1's path question is marked resolved and its trash sub-question is not. Rows 1, 3, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 13, 15, 31 and 33 fail only through such commands (for 6 and 7, also through a non-default autoretrieve mode). So do rows 39, 46 and 54, which also need the trash reading below. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- **Trash.** (superseded 2026-10-04 as to a trash the person selects or confirms, which is now deliberate; the rest of this reading was applied unchanged) Moving another writer's data to Zotero's trash was counted as destroying it where the person did not choose that data. Zotero empties the trash after 30 days by default (defaults/preferences/zotero.js:51 @ ca61760), and production sets no override. Rows 39 and 46 fail on this. The verifiers split on a trash the person selects and confirms, so row 54 carries both outcomes. A permanent erase is destroying (rows 33, 43). §7 item 1's trash sub-question stays open. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- **Rewrite.** (superseded 2026-10-04 as to preview and review dialogs, which are now deliberate; the rest stands) Read literally. A backup line, a preview or review dialog, a conflict check or an alias substitution does not exempt a rewrite. Changes that keep every value (DOI case, Extra whitespace) are not rewrites. Rows 6, 7, 8, 12 and 13 changed on this reading. (Its 2026-10-04 carve-out for preview and review dialogs: superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling.)
- **Key fill.** Writing a key into an item that had none is not a change under (b), because every ruled example changes an existing key (rows 2, 46 and 54, and row 4 with its reservedKeys seed).
- **Annotations.** Annotation comment and colour are not among (a)'s Extra, tags, fields or child notes (row 34).
- **A datum the person names.** (superseded 2026-10-04: a rule bound to an event is judged as the add-on acting by itself, a click is the person's, and an MCP call is remote) A tag the person typed into a rule, or chose to toggle, was not judged as the add-on's rewrite (rows 38, 53). The notes-ui verifier flagged that this line has to be drawn once across rows 38, 44 and 53. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- **Attachment keys.** A new attachment item key is not a citation key (rows 27, 29).

**Configuration.** (superseded 2026-10-04) The seedable-setting pass is ruled for B3/C1 and B4/C5 only. A3 has no configuration clause, so §7 item 2 stays open for it. A result gated by an opt-in pref keeps its gate qualifier (rows 22, 44, 51). Seed keys appear in §4 where a pass rests on a shipped default (row 17), or where a worker named a pref that narrows a fail.

**Results kept on a moved basis.** Cells without ‡ kept their result. Where the basis moved, a verifier refuted it, or a pass rests on a shipped default, §4 carries a "2026-10-03 normalisation" line under the row (rows 1, 3, 4, 10, 15, 17, 21, 22, 26, 27, 29, 44). The earlier A3 bullets in those notes are pre-normalisation.

### Second ruling (2026-10-04): judged on what it does by itself

The operator ruled this principle on 2026-10-04. It supersedes the first pass's invocation readings for A3, each marked "(superseded 2026-10-04)" above. The machine-surface roster and the merge exclusion stand. A second normalisation pass re-judged every A3 cell under it, desk then adversarial verifier per family. Where the verifier refuted the re-judgement, the verifier's result is used and the §4 line says so. § marks a §3 A3 cell whose result this pass changed; † and ‡ keep their meanings.

**What counts.** A component is judged on what it does by itself: on item add or file create, on startup or load, on a schedule or sweep, on open, in the background, through a notifier or event hook reacting to any writer, or remotely. Remote covers a REST, MCP, URI or CLI handler, a sync, a git pull, and any request not made by the person in the app at that moment. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**What does not count.** What the person deliberately does through the component is the person's own edit, the same as editing by hand, and is not judged. That covers a command or menu action the person runs on items or files they selected, a button the person presses, and a bulk change the person confirms in a prompt. A confirm or preview dialog is deliberate whatever its scope. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**Settings.** A component passes if a setting turns the counted behaviour off. For a Zotero add-on that setting is a preference: README.md's "Automatic-mode preference" column is where research-vault declares it, and doctor reports it. For an Obsidian plugin it is a seedable data.json, app.json or core-plugin setting. Each §4 line names the key and value. Disabling the component outright is not a confining setting.

**A3 under the principle.**

- (a) The add-on does not by itself destroy or rewrite another writer's data in Extra, tags, fields or child notes. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- (b) The add-on does not by itself change an existing citation key. Filling an empty key is not a change. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- Merging stays out of A3: it is Zotero's own behaviour (issue #183).

**B3 and C1** become: the plugin does not by itself write into a machine surface, on the roster above. They decide no cell in this screen. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**Formerly unruled readings the principle settles.**

- A CLI, URI or REST handler is remote, so it counts: BBT's JSON-RPC (row 1), Add-on Market's zotero:// routes (row 42), the MCP tools (row 44), and the read-only endpoints of rows 35 and 49.
- A confirm modal is deliberate, so it does not count (rows 12, 13, 15, 33, 46, 51 and 54). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- Sending output outside the vault confines it (B3/C1; no cell here).
- A person choosing a location in a dialog is deliberate (B3/C1; no cell here). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- §7 item 1's trash sub-question: a trash the person selects or confirms is deliberate (rows 46 and 54). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- §7 item 2, for A3: a destructive path that a named setting turns off passes, so the gated fails of rows 22, 44 and 51 become passes with the key in §4.
- A button in a preferences pane is one the person presses (row 39's compressHistory, row 45's library-wide passes, row 51's reclaim and empty-trash). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

**Readings the principle leaves open.** These are the workers' and verifiers' readings, not rulings.

- A library-wide menu command with no selection and no confirm. The citation-tally verifier read it as a sweep, which counts, and row 20's fail rests on that; the re-judgement had read it as the person's act. Other verifiers read the same shape as the person's act where no result turned on it: Metadata Hunter's toolbar button and shortcuts fall back to collections or the whole library with no confirm (row 10), and Attachment Scanner's whole-library Tools scan has no confirm (row 26, where the named settings would confine it either way). open-citations' Tools-menu stale pass is confined by writeExtra=false either way (row 22). (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)
- parentItem as child-note data. Row 2's pass needs a new child's parent to lie outside (a)'s "data in child notes", and no preference confines the \_saveData patch.
- Annotation properties. Row 34 now writes by itself (an annotation sync on a notifier and on PDF open), and its pass rests only on §2a's Annotations reading, which this ruling does not displace.
- Whether expanding a shortDOI to its long DOI is a rewrite (rows 6, 7 and 9). Rows 6 and 7 name autoretrieve=none, so their results do not turn on it.
- A remote route with an arbitrary-write capability and no observed destructive act. Add-on Market's signed execJS (row 42) fails on it; a shipped-code-only reading would pass it, as the first pass did.
- The zotero-plugin-toolkit route zotero://plugin?action=install, bundled by rows 9, 12 and 16, installs an add-on from a URL with no prompt and writes no item data; whether it is reachable from outside Zotero was not checked. The toolkit's debug-bridge route asks for a confirm. No cell turns on either. (At 2026-10-04, second ruling; superseded in two facts by check remote-write-routes, 2026-10-04. The route is registered by the shipped bundles of 21 screened rows, 2, 4, 9, 12, 16, 19, 20, 29, 31, 33, 35, 36, 37, 38, 39, 42, 44, 45, 50, 52 and 54, plus the excluded Better Authors. The checks moved no cell on this bullet's reading; whether the row 42 capability reading above reaches the route is §7 item 38.) (Its reliance on the debug bridge's confirm: superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling; §7 item 54.)

### Third ruling (2026-10-04): trigger-neutral, right by construction

The operator ruled this standard on 2026-10-04. It supersedes the first and second rulings' invocation readings, withdrawing the exemption for a person's deliberate act, and every reading that leaned on a backup, the trash or a history being recoverable; each is marked "(superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)" above. A backup or history that nobody monitors counts for nothing. The machine-surface roster (hooks/pretooluse_guard.py:11-23) and the exclusion of merging (#183) stand. A third normalisation pass re-judged every A3 cell under it, desk then adversarial verifier per family. Where the verifier refuted the re-judgement, the verifier's result is used and the §4 line says so. ∆ marks a §3 A3 cell whose result this pass changed; †, ‡ and § keep their meanings.

**Trigger-neutral.** Every write or removal path a component offers is judged alike, whatever triggers it: automatic, on add, a notifier, a schedule, a remote route, a menu command, a button, or a change confirmed in a preview or dialog. A confirmation, a preview, a backup line, an undo or restore command, or git history changes no result. (superseded 2026-10-04 for selection-scoped commands, fourth ruling)

**A3 for a Zotero add-on**, three parts:

- (1) Writes. Every value it writes into a field, Extra, a tag or a child note either comes from an authoritative source looked up by identifier (a DOI resolved through doi.org, Crossref or DataCite by that DOI; a PMID or PMCID from NCBI by identifier; an ISBN; an arXiv ID), or is a format change that keeps all the information (the case of a case-insensitive identifier, a short DOI expanded to its long DOI, reversible punctuation or dash normalisation). A value obtained by fuzzy matching fails, even when it only fills an empty field: title similarity, a bibliographic-query hit taken without an identifier check, a guess from the title, a heuristic.
- (2) Removals. It blanks, truncates, trashes, deletes or strips only what a definite test shows is invalid (an exact placeholder token, a file that does not exist, a link to nothing) or an exact duplicate of information kept on the same item. A heuristic or pattern-match removal fails, and so does removing something merely redundant, such as a snapshot beside a PDF.
- (3) Identity. It does not change an existing citation key, except where research-vault already detects and repairs the change. lifecycle classifies the note re-keyed and propagate maps the old key to the new one (research_vault/lifecycle.py:92-94; research_vault/propagate.py:78-91 @ b06e212), so a re-key passes. Filling an empty key passes.

**Settings.** A component passes if a setting removes every failing path from every trigger; §4 names each key=value. A failing path still reachable from a menu command or a remote route keeps the cell a fail. (superseded 2026-10-04 for selection-scoped commands, fourth ruling) Disabling the component outright is still not a confining setting.

**B3 and C1** (Obsidian plugins): every write path into a machine surface fails, whoever triggers it, unless an existing research-vault mechanism detects that write, named with a cite (a lint that fails on a modified machine-written line, verify's drift check, the vault commit gate), or a seedable setting removes the path from every trigger. There is no hand-edit exemption. **B4 and C5** are unchanged: they are judged at the configuration research-vault would seed. None of the four decides a cell in this screen.

**Readings applied where the ruling is silent.** These are the workers' and verifiers' readings, not rulings. Each names the cells that turn on it.

- A setting may be a set of keys, some false or strings (rows 3, 4, 12 and 27 need several).
- A value that stops every write while the add-on stays loaded counts as disabling it outright, so it is not a confining setting. Rows 20 and 24 turn on it: a databaseOrder naming no supported database (row 20) and a sourceOrder naming no valid source (row 24) each stop every item write on every trigger, so read otherwise, both pass.
- Values the add-on produces itself (class iv: a marker or status tag, a status line, a JSON record note, OCR or conversion output) and content the person wrote that it carries (class v) are neither looked up by identifier nor format changes, so on the literal text they fail (1). Rows 36, 38 and 50 fail only on this (row 50 also on §7 item 39). Row 26 has no confining setting only because of its ungated #broken status tag, whose test is also a catch-all. Rows 27 and 30 add a setting for theirs. The re-judgement put row 53 here too; the verifier read its by-name tag retraction as a determinate (2) strip, the same shape as row 36's, so row 53 fails either way. Whether row 37 fails only on these classes is not established (§4 row 37).
- A stale-snapshot whole-Extra write-back is a (2) removal, reachable from every trigger that runs it. Row 5 turns on it once pmcid.tags=false is seeded; row 17 fails without it.
- Attachment items, their relations and attachment file content are within (2), as the ruling's snapshot example implies. Rows 27 (the move erases dc:replaces and owl:sameAs), 30 and 32 turn on it. If an overwrite at a computed file name is read as outside (2), row 30 and row 27's copy-overwrite path revert together.
- A new attachment's own title and file are outside (1); copying an existing library value into a new item's field keeps the information.
- The fields of an item the add-on creates are within (1). Rows 14 and 25 turn on it.
- An overwrite with a value from an authoritative lookup by identifier is judged under (1), even where the value carries less than the field held; (2)'s truncation covers blank, truncate and strip operations. Row 12's pass turns on it, because at its confining setting a refresh drops Crossref's subtitle from the title.
- An in-process object an add-on exports initiates nothing and is not a trigger. Rows 3 (ZutiloChrome) and 4 (the arXiv Workflow api object) must be read alike, and both passes turn on it.
- Whether an arbitrary-code route is a (1), (2) or (3) path is §7 item 38. Under this ruling a confirm no longer separates the toolkit debug bridge from row 42's execJS (§7 item 54), so row 4's pass turns on item 38, and so may every toolkit bundler.
- (1)'s identifier list is read as examples, so Semantic Scholar, INSPIRE or OpenAlex queried by DOI or arXiv ID count as lookups by identifier; an add-on replacing its own earlier count line is an update under (1), not a (2) removal; a tag or collection the person types is not a matched value. No cell turns on any of these.
- Still unruled and deciding no cell: whether dateAdded and dateModified are fields under (1); whether collection membership is within (2); whether a child attachment is among (1)'s objects (§7 item 42).

**Tally.** (pre-fourth-ruling) A3 over the 54 rows of §3, after this pass: 14 pass (rows 3, 4, 12, 18, 22, 27, 28, 35, 40, 41, 44, 48, 49 and 52), 40 fail, 0 needs-live. Before it: 50 pass, 4 fail (rows 1, 16, 20 and 42). This pass changed 36 cells, all pass → fail: rows 2, 5–11, 13–15, 17, 19, 21, 23–26, 29–34, 36–39, 43, 45–47, 50, 51, 53 and 54. Rows 1, 16, 20 and 42 kept their fail, rows 1, 16 and 20 on a moved basis. The verifiers refuted one re-judged result (row 30, pass → fail) and the basis or setting of four more (rows 20, 27, 37 and 53).

**Passes that rest on a setting.** (pre-fourth-ruling) Rows 18, 28, 35, 40, 41, 48, 49 and 52 need none.

- On values other than the shipped default: rows 3 (removeTags is shown on production), 4 (three features keys; production holds the shipped true, with the add-on userDisabled), 12 (five keys, one a string that must be seeded) and 27 (file_behavior=copy, which production holds, with five shipped values; production does not hold add_zotmoov_tag=false or copy_overwrite=false).
- On the shipped default: rows 22 (writeExtra=false) and 44 (write.enabled unset or false). Neither is installed on production.

**Fact for the spec, not a result.** §2a's fact above still holds and widens: rows 3, 4, 12 and 27 rest on several keys, some false and one a string above the preferences pane's range, which README's one-key automatic-mode column and doctor's exactly-True test cannot assert.

**§4.** A line beginning "∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling" records this pass for one row. Where one follows, the § line and every A3 bullet before it are pre-third-ruling. Rows whose result and basis this pass left unchanged carry no ∆ line: 18, 28, 35, 40, 41, 42, 44, 48, 49 and 52.

### Fourth ruling (2026-10-04): beyond commands on the selection

The operator ruled this on 2026-10-04. For A3 it supersedes the third ruling's trigger-neutral clause and the sentence of its Settings paragraph that keeps a path reachable from a menu command failing; both are marked "(superseded 2026-10-04 for selection-scoped commands, fourth ruling)" above. Parts (1)–(3) of the third ruling's A3 stand, as the test applied to the paths this ruling counts. The machine-surface roster and the exclusion of merging (#183) stand. A fourth reading re-derived every A3 cell under it, re-derivation then adversarial verifier per family. It started from the failing paths and triggers each row's ∆ line records, and re-opened code only where a recorded path's trigger or scope (selection or library) was unclear. Where the verifier refuted the re-derivation, the verifier's result is used. ◇ marks a §3 A3 cell whose result this reading changed; †, ‡, §, ◊ and ∆ keep their meanings.

**Commands on the selection are not judged.** An add-on is judged only on what it does beyond commands the person runs on items they selected. A menu or toolbar command acting on the person's selection is the Zotero counterpart of editing the open note, and is not judged for the same reason: research-vault's capture monitor, designed in this spec, reports at the next capture every snapshot field that changed or vanished and every attachment that was dropped, as a finding that holds publish until it is acknowledged.

**Counted paths.** Parts (1)–(3) are applied to these:

- automatic writes: on add, item notifiers reacting to any writer, startup, and scheduled or periodic sweeps;
- commands that act on the whole library, a collection or a search rather than the selection, including a command that falls back to the library when nothing is selected;
- remote routes: JSON-RPC, MCP, HTTP endpoints and zotero:// protocol handlers;
- writes the monitor cannot see: files on disk outside the item's own attachment records (writing into a linked file's folder, for example), other items the person did not select, and add-on state outside the item.

The third ruling's other clauses stand for these paths. A confirmation, a preview, a backup line or an undo changes no result, so a library-wide command behind a confirm counts (row 2's bulk merge), and so does a preferences-pane button that acts on the library (rows 39, 45 and 51).

**Settings.** A setting that removes every failing counted path passes; §4 names each key=value. For Zotero that is a preference research-vault can declare: README's add-on table, which doctor checks once #190 is fixed. Disabling the component outright is still not a confining setting.

**Readings applied where the ruling is silent.** These are the re-derivers' and verifiers' readings, not rulings. Each names the cells that turn on it.

- The selection is item-level: the selected or open item together with its own child attachments, the unit the monitor snapshots. Rows 31, 32 and 33 pass on it; on a narrower reading, where a child or parent the person did not select is another item, each fails with no setting. Row 24 draws the same line upward: a selected attachment's regular parent is within the selection, because its Extra is in the capture snapshot; read otherwise, row 24 fails.
- An item an add-on creates is an "other item not selected", because the monitor holds no snapshot of it, and the third ruling's reading that a created item's fields are within (1) applies to it. Rows 11 (the verifier's reading, which refuted the re-derivation's pass), 14 and 25 fail on it; read otherwise, rows 11 and 25 pass, and row 14 still fails because its Import acts with nothing selected. Row 10's item-menu published-version swap is counted on it too, with no change to that fail. Row 46 reads the same shape the other way: a parent Zotero's recognizer builds for a PDF the person ticked is the command's own product, where row 11's verifier counts the parent Create Parent Item builds for a selected PDF. Read alike, either row 11 passes or row 46 fails on the recognizer's title-match parent (§7 item 52). Rows 30 and 32 treat the empty parent they create for a selected standalone PDF as part of the selection; row 32's carries only the attachment's title, and no result turns on either.
- Groups the person ticks in an add-on's own scan panel are a selection, each tick a choice of one displayed group of existing items, while a folder the person picks is container-shaped (recursive, by extension), like a collection. Row 46 passes on the first; row 50 is counted on the second.
- A click on the open reader item, or on an item-scope goal row, selects that item (row 53).
- Code the person configured, run by the person's own command, is the person's act (rows 37 and 38). Row 38's setting excludes script and triggerAction rules on every trigger anyway, which is broader than this reading needs.
- A two-step command on the selection is one command: Zutilo's relocateChildren writes children copied earlier into a selected parent. It is row 3's only reading dependency; read as a write to unselected items, it must be read alike with row 2's \_saveData patch, which turns on the open parentItem reading.
- A value that stops every write, the unjudged selection command included, still counts as disabling outright. Row 20 turns on it: a databaseOrder naming no supported database would also stop the counted Tools retally, and read otherwise, row 20 passes.
- Row 9's pass rests on a property of Zotero 10.0.5 read from code, not live-tested: the collection-menu context carries no items, so the rule menus it registers there write nothing. On a Zotero that passes items to that context, row 9 fails with no setting.
- Row 50's fail now rests on §7 item 39 alone: the verifier found its class iv basis conflicts with the third ruling's reading that a new attachment's own title and file lie outside (1). If this ruling's "a preference research-vault can declare" is read as settling item 39, extensions.zotero.autoRecognizeFiles=false confines the recognizer path while imports keep working, and row 50 passes.

**Third-ruling readings after this ruling.** On counted paths these still decide cells: class iv and v (row 36 fails only on its marker add once removetags=addonly is set, and row 26 on its ungated #broken tag together with its catch-all test); a computed-name overwrite within (2) (row 30 fails on it and passes if it is read as outside (2); rows 27 and 33 name settings that remove theirs); an authoritative overwrite judged under (1) (row 12); and a created item's fields within (1) (above). Whether an arbitrary-code route is a (1), (2) or (3) path is still §7 item 38, with item 54: every toolkit bundler's result turns on it, row 42 fails on it, and rows 4, 9, 12, 31, 33, 35, 37, 38, 44 and 52 name it in their passes. No cell now turns on the in-process-object reading (rows 3 and 4 need no setting), on a sourceOrder naming no valid source (row 24 needs no such lever) or on the stale-snapshot write-back (row 5's notifier is removed by its setting).

**Results kept.** Rows whose result this reading kept carry no ◇ line. Where the deciding path or setting moved, it is named here, with the verifier's cite at the row's pin where it is new.

- Fails, each on a counted path that no named setting removes:
  - 1: the File-menu AUX scan to a tag strips the named tag from every uncited item in every library (its query has no library clause, and nothing gates the command), on a test that is not definite (2). The ∆ line calls its remaining fails gated only by a selection, which is wrong for this path; its other menu fails act on the selection and drop out, and its automatic key fills and re-keys and the remote item.regenerate_key pass (3). The verifier lists counted paths no pass has judged, none moving the cell: tagDuplicates' own marker tag across a library, the remote autoexport.add writing to a path the caller supplies, an export's removal of its target directory, and the opt-in auto-export git checkout (content/better-bibtex.ts:579-594; content/aux-scanner.ts:96, 313-333; content/key-manager.ts:532-596; content/json-rpc.ts:82-112; content/worker/zotero.ts:326-330; content/auto-export.ts:230-231 @ f3c9299).
  - 2: the bulk merge acts on every duplicate group in the library, and its pre-merge overlay fills empty master fields from siblings (1); duplicate.default.action=cancel removes only the on-add triggers (src/features/bulkMerge/bulkMergeService.ts:245-311; src/shared/duplicates/mergeItems.ts:10-29 @ 1d95191).
  - 10: the Tools, toolbar and shortcut entries fall back to the selected collections or libraries with no confirm, through the title race (1) and the published-version trash (2).
  - 11: the verifier refuted the re-derivation's pass. Create Parent Item writes a new top-level item the person did not select, with DOI, archiveID and url guessed by pattern from the PDF's full-text cache, then runs the metadata updater on it (1); no preference removes it (chrome/content/zotmeta.js:481-512; chrome/content/utilities.js:9-10, 526-612 @ v2.1 asset sha256 2a20b823…).
  - 13: both normaliser dialogs act on a collection or the whole library (1).
  - 14: Import creates items from the vendor's matches with nothing selected (1).
  - 15: the collection menu, the Tools run's fallback to the current collection, and undo by key (1)+(2).
  - 16: the delete observer, reacting to any writer, erases Citations child notes on unselected items (2).
  - 17: the Tools sync runs with forceRun over every library, bypassing autoUpdateOnStartup (1)+(2).
  - 19: the load sweep, the add notifier and the collection menu write title-matched notes (1); it has no preferences.
  - 20: the whole-library Tools retally strips other writers' count lines (2), on the disabling reading above.
  - 25: Add writes a new item from a title-matched hit (1), on the created-item reading above. Its only preference is extensions.zotero.semanticzotero.relateItems (the verifier's correction of the key), which removes only the relation write.
  - 26: the Tools whole-library scan adds and strips #broken on a catch-all test, with no gate (1).
  - 29: the collection-menu Attach New File runs Zotero's recognizer on a new standalone item, with no gate (1).
  - 30: tesseract output lands in a linked PDF's folder with no existence check (2), on the computed-name reading.
  - 34: the reference-panel import creates a new item from an OpenAlex search match (1). The verifier adds that the Obsidian export rewrites, with no prompt, a note whose frontmatter the person edited (2), so the cell fails on the export even if a picked reference counts as the selection (bootstrap.js:124217-124238, 124784-124791, 125010-125017 @ v0.4.2 asset sha256 89593072…).
  - 36: the startup and library-wide syncs add the class iv marker; removetags=addonly removes only the strip.
  - 39: compressHistory, a preferences-pane button, iterates every main item and trashes on a parse failure (2).
  - 42: the execJS protocol route, on §7 item 38.
  - 43: the collection-menu sync skips the sync-config gate and erases Notion-URL attachments by pattern (2).
  - 45: two preferences-pane buttons act on the whole user library (2).
  - 50: the folder import, which the verifier places in the File menu with a selected collection required, runs Zotero's recognizer (1), on §7 item 39 alone (above) (content/folder-import.ts:172-191, 257-337 @ a23ea42).
  - 51: the empty-trash and reclaim preferences-pane buttons (2).
  - 54: the Tools-menu, onboarding and collection checks overwrite a matched personal item's whole Extra (1)+(2).
- Passes kept on a moved basis:
  - 3 and 4: every write is a command on the selection, so neither pass rests on a setting any more; row 4's still turns on §7 item 38.
  - 12: the five keys stand for the collection, search and library refreshes; Restore and Fetch citation counts act on the selection and leave the counted set.
  - 27: the verifier refuted the setting. Besides file_behavior=copy, copy_overwrite=false, custom_menu_items='{}' and attach_search_dir='', it needs extensions.zotmoov.menu_items.convert_linked.hidden=true with keys.convert_linked='' (Convert linked to stored moves a selected linked file's source with no check for another attachment linking the same path), and delete_files=false (production holds it) or prune_empty_dir=false (the delete queue's prune removes desktop.ini, Thumbs.db and .DS_Store by filename) (src/02-zotmoov.js:139-151, 530-535, 580-587; src/03-zotmoov-menus-new.js:13, 117-130, 157; prefs.js:23, 25 @ 1.2.32 xpi sha256 7770dc2c…).

## 3. Results

Each cell shows the verified result. A2 cells name the step(s), with "—" where none is served. † marks a must where the verifier refuted the desk (`refuted: true`), either by changing the result or by refuting the desk's basis or write list while the result stood. §4 gives the desk result and the reason. Where the verifier corrected an exit line, the corrected line is shown here and the desk's line is in §4.

## 4. Per-candidate notes

Each note gives the full pin, the installed state or A1 ranges, the write targets with cites, and every point where the verifier disagreed with the desk. Cites are file:line at the candidate's pin unless another pin is named. "Zotero @ ca61760" means zotero/zotero at the 10.0.5 tag commit; the verifier checked it against the installed omni.ja. "BBT @ f3c9299" means Better BibTeX v9.0.63.

### 4.1 identity-hygiene

**1. Better BibTeX.**

- **Pin.** The installed xpi (profile extensions/better-bibtex@iris-advies.com.xpi, sha256 3a4d080ec94153a8...) matches tag v9.0.63 @ f3c9299e1f8684df96b8ddf116c3880da1cdde7f. HEAD 71cec8067843bc5cdcbac7e1e794a3803638a5e1 is one test-only commit past the v9.0.68 tag (1af0bfe, released 2026-09-30, asset manifest 8.0.1-10.\*). The merge shim content/better-bibtex.ts:96-163 is byte-identical at both. Licence: MIT per LICENSE:1 at f3c9299, while package.json:7 says ISC; the mismatch is already recorded at import-sourcing.md:4046 (§28).
- **Installed.** 9.0.63, active=true, userDisabled=false, appDisabled=false (extensions.json addons[id=better-bibtex@iris-advies.com]). compatibility.ini:2 LastVersion=10.0.5_20260930221031; C:/Program Files/Zotero/app/application.ini Version=10.0.5.
  - extraMergeCitekeys, extraMergeTeX and extraMergeCSL ship false (xpi defaults/preferences/defaults.js:54-56) and are unset in prefs.js. resetKeyOnChange ships false (defaults.js:46) and is unset.
  - Two auto-exports are enabled (prefs.js:133-134). One is a library-wide Better CSL JSON export to kh-vault/system/bibliography.json, mtime 2026-10-03 17:42.
- **A2 cites.** research_vault/capture.py:73-78, :166; research_vault/zotero.py:345-347 (library route), :465 (json-rpc), :491-509; README.md:123. The vault calls only api.ready, item.attachments and item.export (zotero.py:492-505).
- **Automatic writes.**
  - The native citationKey field for unkeyed items (key-manager.ts:311-318, :449-463). Existing keys are kept (:449).
  - Clones get a cleared citationKey (better-bibtex.ts:89-93).
  - The merge shim rewrites the target's Extra through Extra.set, but only when Zotero.Items.merge is called and an extraMerge\* pref is on (better-bibtex.ts:96-163; extra.ts:203-245).
  - Auto-export files at configured paths (worker/zotero.ts:458; auto-export.ts:85).
  - better-bibtex/read-only.json in the data directory (key-manager.ts:133-160; measured at 2 bytes).
  - The one-time migration (key-manager/migrate.ts:177-185, 224).
  - Translators written into the data directory (translators.ts:475).
- **User-invoked writes.**
  - fill (regenerate or refresh) re-emits the tex.ids alias (key-manager.ts:213-256, alias at :233-244; extra.ts:207-209). The verifier corrected the desk here: alias re-emission happens inside fill, which is reached only from the menu or JSON-RPC (better-bibtex.ts:653, :659, :665; json-rpc.ts:483-512). It is not automatic.
  - The #duplicate-citation-key tag (key-manager.ts:532-595).
  - patchDates (menu-helper.ts:20-59), applyCitationKeyFromExtra (:79-96), sentenceCase (:98-120) and addCitationLinks (:128-142).
  - The AUX scan (aux-scanner.ts:272-330).
  - JSON-RPC regenerate_key (json-rpc.ts:483) and autoexport.add (:82).
  - Import translators (translators/bibtex/bibtex.ts:1078, 1233, 1262, 1579-1603).
  - The error-report zip (ErrorReport.ts:279).
- **A4† (verifier additions; all close from code).**
  - Git checkout, pull, add, commit and push inside an export target's repository when that repository opts in through .git/config (auto-export.ts:180-254). The mode defaults to config (defaults.js:64) and is absent from prefs.js. kh-vault has no opt-in (measured).
  - Collections created by autoexport.add (collection.ts:72-76) and by BibTeX and BBT-JSON imports (bibtex.ts:272-281; bbtjson.ts:174-194).
  - The editable citation-key column (better-bibtex.ts:852-856).
  - Exports with files copy attachments and recursively remove the snapshot target directory (worker/zotero.ts:321-340).
  - A Lua filter file in the BBT data directory, with old copies deleted (aux-scanner.ts:150-160).
  - The AUX scan removes collection items and tags (:281-286, :325-331). Its report note is standalone, not a child note (:298-301).
  - Automatic pref writes (formatter.ts:420-421, path-search.ts:12, auto-export.ts:289-290, migrate.ts:170).

- § 2026-10-04 by-itself: fail† → fail† (result kept; basis moved) — counted: the JSON-RPC method item.regenerate_key at /better-bibtex/json-rpc is a remote request. It runs KeyManager.fill with replace on and warn left at its default false, so the bulk-modify confirm is never reached; any local client can re-key an existing item with no prompt, and the old key is recorded nowhere, which fails (b). The endpoint registers unconditionally at startup and no BBT preference gates it. The only switch that would remove it is Zotero's own HTTP server, which is not the add-on's preference, and research-vault's capture posts to this same endpoint (research_vault/zotero.py:465), so no confining setting exists. The installed 9.0.63 xpi ships the method and its parameter schema (content/better-bibtex.js, local read 2026-10-04). Automatic paths do not fail: the items-changed handler passes replace=resetKeyOnChange and update returns early when a key exists, fillMissing and the autofill scheduler fill only empty keys, the startup migration fills only where the native key is empty unless the person resolves conflicts in a dialog (choice.dynamic ships false, so it cannot turn resetKeyOnChange on), and the clone patch clears only the new clone's key. The remote scanAUX can target only a collection, never a tag, and changes membership plus a new standalone report note, outside (a)'s list. sentenceCase, applyCitationKeyFromExtra, patchDates, the AUX scan to a tag, regenerate, refresh and tagDuplicates are reached only from menu handlers, so they are the person's own edits. The same remote fill also removes the new key from the Extra aliases line, BBT's own tex.ids grammar, which is not an (a) hit. Setting for the automatic re-key on change: extensions.zotero.translators.better-bibtex.resetKeyOnChange=false (shipped default; unset on production). Re-judgement and verifier agree (content/json-rpc.ts:64-67, :483, :512, :787; content/key-manager.ts:186, :191, :233-244, :389, :450; content/key-manager/migrate.ts:57-63, :146-157, :170, :176; content/better-bibtex.ts:89-93, :611, :653-705, :827; content/aux-scanner.ts:280-301; content/Preferences/preferences.yaml:409-412 @ f3c9299; Zotero duplicatesMergePane.js:195, :205-210 @ ca61760).
- ¤ 2026-10-04 check remote-write-routes: A3 fail† → fail† (result kept) — (b) fails and (a) does not. The remote JSON-RPC method item.regenerate_key looks up the given citekeys and calls KeyManager.fill with replace on; warn stays at its default false, so the warnBulkModify confirm is skipped, and update() writes the proposed key over any existing native citationKey, a hand-set one included, whenever the two differ. The old key goes back to the caller and is stored nowhere. The installed 9.0.63 xpi (sha256 3a4d080e…) ships the method in content/better-bibtex.js (re-read 2026-10-04). /better-bibtex/json-rpc is registered by the orchestrator task at startup; neither that task, the orchestrator nor the handler reads a preference, the handler accepts GET and POST, and preferences.yaml has no rpc, server, endpoint or http key, so citekeyFormat, keyScope, warnBulkModify, resetKeyOnChange and the other key preferences do not gate this path. Zotero 10.0.5 cancels browser-shaped requests (§7 item 32); any non-browser local client gets through. Its other remote routes stay outside (a) and (b): scanAUX clears and refills a collection and adds a standalone note, never a tag; autoexport.add creates a collection and an export; the pull-export and CAYW endpoints are reads or the person's own picker. BBT bundles no zotero-plugin-toolkit, so it has no zotero://plugin route. The only switch that removes the endpoint is Zotero's own extensions.zotero.httpServer.enabled, which is not a BBT preference and which capture's route needs; whether a host-application preference can be a confining setting is §7 item 39. Rows 1 and 44 are read the same way and differ only through the settings clause (content/json-rpc.ts:64-67, 82-112, 483-512, 709-760, 764-792; content/key-manager.ts:186-191, 212-217, 428-461; content/server.ts:1-17; content/aux-scanner.ts:280-310; content/Preferences/preferences.yaml (no gating key); content/better-bibtex.ts:31-32 @ f3c9299; installed xpi content/better-bibtex.js regenerate_key, read 2026-10-04; research_vault/zotero.py:464-468 @ worktree HEAD 8cedd18)

Setting: none; no preference gates those three. extensions.zotero.translators.better-bibtex.patchDates="" (shipped as a four-mapping list) removes only patchDates, whose re-emission of the remaining tex.\* lines with prefix and key lower-cased is itself a lossy (1) rewrite (the verifier's addition). Passing: key fills, the clone-key clear, and fill's removal of the new key from the aliases line. Verifier corrections, none moving the cell: the move variant drops every matching Extra line and keeps only the last match's value, in the native key; with auxImport=true (ships false) the menu and remote AUX scans also import entries from the .bib; remote scanAUX in replace mode clears the target collection's members, and whether collection membership is within (2) is unruled; fill also collapses every tex., bibtex. and biblatex. ids line and Citation Key Alias line into one sorted tex.ids line, not strictly lossless. Also unruled: the clone patch fires inside Zotero's moveToLibrary, so an item moved to another library gets a new key and lifecycle reads the note as deleted. On this profile every Zoplicate merge is user-run, so the fail rests on the all-paths reading.

(b): no existing key changes. The overlay spreads the losers' JSON under the master's, and toJSON omits a blank key, so an unkeyed master can receive a loser's key. That is a fill; the verifier corrected the re-judgement's claim that no path writes citationKey. (a): the cancel path un-trashes only the items that run trashed. The always-on \_saveData patch points a new child of a trashed parent (another writer's note or attachment) at the duplicate group's master. The pass reads that as no rewrite, because Zotero holds parentItem as a primary property outside (a)'s list. If that reading is rejected, the cell fails under every path reading, since the patch is automatic (src/shared/duplicates/mergeItems.ts:16-29 (:24-25); src/integrations/zotero/patches/patchItemSaveData.ts:24-36; src/features/bulkMerge/bulkMergeService.ts:302, :309, :316-320 @ 1d95191). - § 2026-10-04 by-itself: pass‡ → pass‡ (result kept; basis moved) — counted: (i) on an item add by any writer, whenItemsAdded acts on duplicates. With duplicate.default.action keep or discard it merges with no prompt; merging is out of A3, and its pre-merge overlay only fills the master's empty fields, an empty key included, which is a fill. With ask it opens a dialog, which is deliberate; with cancel it returns at once. Adds that arrive by remote sync are skipped, and local adds made during a sync are deferred and flushed afterwards, still gated by the action preference (the verifier's correction of the re-judgement's "skipped during sync"). (ii) The always-on \_saveData patch moves a brand-new child, saved under a trashed parent that has duplicates, to the group's master; existing children are never touched, and it writes no note content, Extra, tags, fields or key. The bulk merge and its cancel/untrash path are buttons the person presses. Setting for (i): extensions.zotero.zoplicate.duplicate.default.action=cancel (production prefs.js:146) or ask (shipped default, addon/prefs.js:2). No preference confines (ii): it passes only on the first pass's reading that parentItem lies outside (a)'s "data in child notes", which the second ruling does not settle, and if that reading is rejected the cell fails. The non-strict fromJSON migration of Extra lines into fields is the call Zotero's own merge pane makes, so it is treated as part of Zotero's merge; §7 item 10's live leg is still pending (src/features/duplicates/duplicates.ts:194-207, :263; src/features/duplicates/notifyHandlers.ts:179-182, :226-235; src/shared/duplicates/mergeItems.ts:16-29; src/integrations/zotero/patches/patchItemSaveData.ts:24-36; src/features/bulkMerge/bulkMergeService.ts:302, :319-320; addon/prefs.js:2 @ 1d95191; Zotero duplicatesMergePane.js:203-211 @ ca61760; production prefs.js:146).

Setting: none; duplicate.default.action=cancel (production prefs.js:146) removes only the on-add trigger, and no preference hides the button or confines the patch. Passing: the overlay's citationKey fill (3), the cancel path's untrash of its own run's items, and the lossless Extra-to-field migration. **4. arXiv Workflow.**

- **Pin.** The installed xpi (sha256 b5e35372fcd805d4...; bundle content/scripts/arxiv-workflow.js) matches v0.3.9 @ 68dc716d386a2fbf4b25bcf8d611e13f3b05bed7. HEAD 6adc06879be6e9d52c7e24e27ff21854c9866d7b = v0.4.0 (2026-10-01). The asset zotero-arxiv-workflow.xpi manifest and repo addon/manifest.json:16-17 both say 7.999-10.\*. arxiv-merge.ts, prefer-pdf.ts and update-pdf.ts are unchanged since v0.3.9. Licence AGPL-3.0 (LICENSE:1-2; package.json:32; import-sourcing.md:5408).
- **Installed.** 0.3.9, active=false, userDisabled=true, appDisabled=false. The live profile overrides merge.arXivExtra=true and merge.trashUnannotatedPDF=true (prefs.js:58-59); both ship false (xpi prefs.js:13, :15). merge.reservedKeys is at its default (xpi prefs.js:12).
- **Merge writes (arxiv-merge.ts).**
  - The surviving preprint's item type is changed (:159).
  - Every non-reserved field is replaced from the published item via non-strict fromJSON, including the native citationKey, and absent fields are cleared (:160, :164-173, :218-219; item.js:5969-5975, 6019-6025 @ ca61760).
  - Extra is combined through combineExtraFields. arXiv: lines are stripped unless arXivExtra is on (:179-195).
  - The published item gets a random-UUID Citation Key Extra line and is saved (:211-217).
  - A linked-URL attachment to the arXiv URL is added when there is no snapshot (:224-238).
  - With trashUnannotatedPDF on, unannotated preprint PDFs are trashed (:239-247).
  - The published PDFs' dateAdded is backdated (:249-266).
  - Finally Zotero.Items.merge runs (:270).
- **Other writes.**
  - Update: a new item via a translator into the selected collections (manager.ts:18-60 HEAD; :94 v0.3.9), and a PDF via addAvailableFile with its title set (manager.ts:298-305 HEAD; :231-238 v0.3.9).
  - update-pdf.ts:47 and prefer-pdf.ts:53-55.
  - Prefs.
- **Verifier additions.**
  - The same mergeItems.mjs effects as Zoplicate (mergeItems.mjs:98-106, :130-203, :453-474).
  - The UUID Citation Key line stays on the trashed published item.
  - The merge.arXivURL pref governs url (xpi prefs.js:14; arxiv-merge.ts:175).
- **A2.** importPaper (manager.ts:276-310); import-sourcing.md:5357. Every path is user-initiated: there is no notifier, only a pref observer (hooks.ts:42).
- **A3 fail.** The survivor is re-keyed outside BBT, and its former key is neither kept nor aliased.
  - The :270 merge does reach the shim, but the shim's prefs are off. Even with them on, the shim would alias only the loser's key, which now equals the survivor's (better-bibtex.ts:145-148).
  - The comment at :197-209 assumes BBT regenerates keys on save, which resetKeyOnChange=false prevents.
  - The verifier made three corrections:
    - lifecycle.py:91 is the trashed-attachment branch and :94 is 're-keyed'. With trashUnannotatedPDF live, a note that recorded the trashed PDF classifies as 'trashed' first.
    - If the published item is still unkeyed when it is serialised, fromJSON clears the preprint's citationKey and BBT generates a fresh one (key-manager.ts:389-393, :449-463 @ f3c9299).
    - "Filtered out" depends on BBT's key cache, which is delayed by fillKeyAfter=2.
  - Either way the old key is lost.
- **Desk exit line.** Same as the corrected line, without the clause about the UUID line on trashed items.
- 2026-10-03 normalisation: fail kept, on a moved basis; it fails (b) on every path reading. Before Zotero's merge, the add-on's own fromJSON loads the published item's JSON, citationKey included, into the surviving preprint, because reservedKeys ships without citationKey. The survivor takes the published key, or, if the published item is unkeyed, is cleared and re-keyed by BBT. Zotero's merge alone would keep the master's key, so the person asked for a merge, not a re-key. (a) also fails: that fromJSON replaces every non-reserved field and clears fields the published item lacks, and arXiv: Extra lines drop unless merge.arXivExtra is on. The UUID Citation Key line goes onto the published item, which is then trashed. BBT reads that grammar only at export when the native key is empty, or through the menu command, so the line does not affect identity (src/modules/arxiv-merge.ts:159-173, :189-193, :211-219, :270; addon/prefs.js:12-16; package.json:11 @ 68dc716; Zotero item.js:5969-5975 @ ca61760; BBT translators/bibtex/exporter.ts:63-70 @ f3c9299). Seed keys that narrow it without changing the result: extensions.zotero.arxiv-workflow.merge.reservedKeys=collections,dateAdded,dateModified,key,tags,relations,citationKey leaves only a key fill, so the cell rests on (a) through a command the person runs; extensions.zotero.arxiv-workflow.merge.arXivExtra=true, already set on production (prefs.js:58), keeps arXiv: lines. Listing every field in reservedKeys would stop the clearing at :218, but it would also turn the merge into a fill.
- § 2026-10-04 by-itself: fail → pass — counted: none. onStartup waits for Zotero and calls onMainWindowLoad, which registers right-click menus behind the features.\* preferences and one preference observer for update.concurrency; src/ has no notifier, endpoint, interval or startup sweep. Merge needs exactly two selected items, one preprint and one published. Update imports the published version as a new item and merges it into the selected preprint inside the person's command, and prefer-PDF and update-PDF act on the selection. So the survivor's re-key, the field clearing, the dropped arXiv: Extra lines and the UUID Citation Key line all happen inside commands the person runs. src/addon.ts exposes an in-process api object on the Zotero global; it does nothing by itself and is not a REST, URI, MCP or CLI handler (verifier addition). Setting: none needed; merge.reservedKeys and extensions.zotero.arxiv-workflow.merge.arXivExtra=true (production prefs.js:58) narrow the person's command, but the pass does not rest on them. At 68dc716 update's import-then-merge is at manager.ts:204-243, not the manager.ts lines cited above for v0.3.9. Re-judgement and verifier agree (src/hooks.ts:11-48; src/modules/arxiv-merge.ts:20-43; src/modules/arxiv-update/index.ts:30-37; src/modules/arxiv-update/manager.ts:221-223, :243; src/modules/prefer-pdf.ts:18-24; src/modules/update-pdf.ts:19-25; addon/prefs.js:1-20 @ 68dc716).
- ◊ 2026-10-04 live: A4, writes by itself (startup and on-add): pass — the v0.3.9 asset (sha256 b5e35372…9a2a) is the pin above; it loaded active, appDisabled=false. The fixtures included a preprint/published pair (fx2: preprint, arXiv DOI, citekey vaswani2017attention, a ZSCC line and a manual tag; fx3: conferencePaper, citekey vaswani2017neurips, a count line) and a PDF. After 60 s the library rows matched the control after normalisation, and the library version did not move during the wait. Beyond the control it wrote only its xpi, its extensions.json entry and an extensions.webextensions.uuids entry, plus addonStartup.json.lz4, the add-on manager's cache, which changes in every leg (verifier addition) (step2/arxiv-workflow/cmp-control.txt, dbdiff.txt, fsdiff.txt, fxrun.log).
- ◊ 2026-10-04 live: A4, the §6.2 merge leg: needs-live still — the merge is a command the person runs. Human step in §6.4; the state before it is step2/arxiv-workflow/api/find-04-after-wait.json.
- ¤ 2026-10-04 check remote-write-routes: A3 pass§ → pass§ (result kept) — its shipped bundle, matched to this row's pin by manifest id and version, registers the toolkit's zotero://plugin route (an install with no prompt) and zotero://ztoolkit-debug, which this row's lines did not name. The install route writes no item data, so the cell does not move under §2a's toolkit-route reading (§7 item 38), and the debug bridge keeps its confirm. (installed xpi arxiv-workflow.js; bundle scans, 2026-10-04)
- ∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling: pass§ → pass§ (result kept; basis moved: the pass now rests on settings) — Merge fails: setType to the published type runs before the preprint's JSON is read, so it clears every preprint field the new type lacks whatever merge.reservedKeys says (2); fromJSON then loads a library sibling paired only by the selection and a title test (1)+(2); arXiv: Extra lines drop unless merge.arXivExtra=true (2); a random-UUID Citation Key line is written (1); and with merge.trashUnannotatedPDF=true (production prefs.js:59) unannotated preprint PDFs are trashed as merely redundant (2). Update fails (1): its DBLP and PubMed sources are title queries taken without an identifier check, and it then runs the same merge whatever features.arXivMerge says. Prefer PDF overwrites the attachment's url with the parent's (1). Setting: extensions.zotero.arxiv-workflow.features.arXivMerge=false, features.arXivUpdate=false and features.preferPDF=false, which unregister the three menu commands at window load. Each ships true, and production prefs.js holds no features line, so production runs the shipped true; the add-on is userDisabled there. No narrower keys suffice. Passing: the survivor's re-key (3), and Update PDF, which adds a publisher PDF found by DOI. These settings also unregister the commands row 4's A2 credit rests on. The pass turns on two open readings: the in-process Zotero.arXivWorkflow.api object, read alike with row 3's ZutiloChrome; and §7 item 38, because the bundled zotero://ztoolkit-debug bridge runs caller-supplied JavaScript and no add-on preference removes it (§7 item 54). research-vault's two re-key limits do not bear on the pass, because the settings remove the merge (§7 item 49). Re-judgement and verifier agree (src/hooks.ts:29-49; src/modules/arxiv-merge.ts:146-173, :175-195, :211-219, :239-247; src/modules/arxiv-update/manager.ts:204-243; src/modules/arxiv-update/paper-finder.ts:80-238; src/modules/prefer-pdf.ts:40-57; src/modules/update-pdf.ts:36-56; src/addon.ts:33-38; addon/prefs.js:2-18 @ 68dc716; v0.3.9 asset content/scripts/arxiv-workflow.js:214-285, :5430-5433; production prefs.js:58-59)

### 4.2 identifiers-metadata

**5. PMCID fetcher.**

- **Pin.** Release v1.0.3 asset zotero-pmcid-fetcher-1.0.3.xpi, sha256 01b30c1a5aa38b0591e61e41775592b9e383eb67b429cb3afa3a79c7f7766164, byte-identical to the installed copy. HEAD 24b66f88634590748f84dc0f49bc13446f47a6de (2026-08-24). No licence: no LICENSE file at 24b66f8 and no package.json field. Nothing is quoted.
- **Installed.** addons[13], active=true, appDisabled=false. prefs.js:36 lastAppVersion=10.0.5. prefs.js:113-115: auto=true, bundleSize=0, tags=true.
- **Writes.**
  - PMID and PMCID go to native fields where the type has them. In schema 44 only journalArticle does (zotero-schema 70c3aa9 schema.json:1646-1649, which the verifier found byte-identical to the 10.0.5 omni.ja schema).
  - Otherwise it appends one key-value Extra line per identifier. It never overwrites (lib.js:217-223, 246, 327-331).
  - Extra is set whole, from the snapshot taken at job start plus the appended lines (lib.js:229, 370).
  - With tags on, MeSH descriptor names and PubMed keywords become manual type-0 tags (lib.js:350-363; Zotero item.js:4805-4806).
  - Prefs (lib.js:139-142, 344-349).
  - A Help-menu debug upload to filebin.net, on click only (bootstrap.js:2311, 2337, 2356-2370, 2465-2468).
- **§15 item 05.** Answered from code as "both": identifiers go to the native field or to Extra, and tags carry only subject terms. This matches the lane-0 measurement of zero identifier-shaped tags.
- **A2 caveat.** SNAPSHOT_FIELDS (literature_notes.py:34-52) has no PMID or PMCID, so journalArticle identifiers do not reach the literature note.
- **A3 needs-live.** Extra is snapshotted at job start and written back whole after up to three throttled NCBI slots of at least 400 ms each (lib.js:34-42, 229, 370). This runs on both add and modify (155-156), so a concurrent Extra write inside that window is lost. Modify also re-runs efetch for items with a PMID but no PMCID, so MeSH tags the user deleted come back (246, 343, 353-363).
- § 2026-10-04 by-itself: needs-live → pass — counted: an item notifier fires on add and modify from any writer and runs the fetch, which snapshots Extra at job start and writes it back whole after throttled NCBI requests, so a concurrent Extra write in that window can be lost; with tags on it also re-adds MeSH tags. The handler returns first thing unless the auto preference is truthy. Its other startup code runs no fetch: a bundleSize fix, a 400 ms interval that only releases throttle slots, and a Help-menu debug sender. The menu fetch acts on selected items, the person's own edit. Setting: extensions.zotero.pmcid.auto=false. The asset ships no prefs.js, so the shipped state is unset and reads as off, and production sets true (prefs.js:113); the re-judgement said the shipped value is true, and the verifier corrected it. This is README's declared automatic-mode key, and doctor.py:276-280 @ 3780fb7 reports a failure unless it is exactly True. With auto=false seeded, the §6.2 A3 leg no longer decides A3; it would still show what auto=true does. No licence: paraphrased (lib.js:34-42, 130-133, 139-142, 150-168 (gate 153), 181-196, 224-229, 370; prefs.xhtml:12 @ v1.0.3 asset sha256 01b30c1a…).
- ∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling: pass§ → fail — (2): the job snapshots Extra at its start and writes it back whole, with its own lines appended, after up to three throttled NCBI requests on one shared 400 ms queue, so an Extra change another writer or the person saved in that window is reverted with no definite test (§7 item 46). (1): with tags on, an item that reaches the tag fetch with a PMCID but no PMID is fetched from PubMed with the PMCID as its id, which PubMed reads as an unrelated PMID, and that article's MeSH headings and keywords become tags; reproduced live on 2026-10-04 (§7 item 47). Triggers: the item notifier on add and modify from any writer, and the item-menu command on the selection, which reads no preference. Setting: none for every trigger; extensions.zotero.pmcid.auto=false removes only the notifier, and extensions.zotero.pmcid.tags=false removes (1) from both triggers. Its PMID and PMCID writes are by identifier and pass. The verifier notes that once tags=false is seeded, the fail rests only on reading the stale write-back as a (2) removal (§2a, third ruling readings); if that reading is rejected, the cell passes with tags=false. Re-judgement and verifier agree (lib.js:34-42, 130-133, 150-168, 181-196, 224-246, 274-302, 304-340, 341-373 @ v1.0.3 asset sha256 01b30c1a…; no licence, paraphrased)
- ◇ 2026-10-04 fourth ruling: fail§∆ → pass — counted: only the item notifier on add and modify, which fires for any writer. It fails (2) through the stale whole-Extra write-back and, with tags on, (1) through the tag fetch that sends a PMCID as a PMID, and it returns first thing unless pmcid.auto is truthy. The item-menu entry targets main/library/item and fetches for the selection (getSelectedItems filtered to regular items), with no collection or library fallback, so it is not judged. Startup only fixes the bundleSize preference and starts a 400 ms throttle interval, and the Help-menu sender writes no item data. Setting: extensions.zotero.pmcid.auto=false, which leaves the menu command writing, so it is not disabling. README already declares this key, but production holds true, and doctor's exactly-True test cannot assert false until #190 is fixed. Re-derivation and verifier agree (lib.js:130-133, 150-168 (gate 153), 181-196 (onCommand 193), 224-226 @ v1.0.3 asset sha256 01b30c1a…, no licence, paraphrased; Zotero 10.0.5 omni.ja (sha256 e8aba3eb…) menuManager.js:39-40; production prefs.js:113)

- ‡ 2026-10-03 normalisation: pass → fail — short mode ships as a menu command and as an autoretrieve option. It replaces another writer's long DOI with a shortDOI alias handle and keeps the long DOI nowhere on the item, which is a rewrite under (a) on the all-paths reading. Lower-casing in long and check modes keeps the case-insensitive identifier and is not a rewrite. Tag removal touches only its three pref strings, and it has no citation-key code. The re-judgement returned pass on the drafts' same-identifier carve-out; the verifier refuted it, and its fail is used. Under an automatic-only reading at shipped defaults (autoretrieve=long) it would pass (lib/doi-updater.js:241-243, 66-70; lib/doi-service.js:104-117, 126-131; lib/menus.js:19, 60-61; zoteroshortdoi.js:53-66; prefs.js:1-4 @ v1.6.0 asset sha256 a52a846d…, tag object 23fd543 → 2ec547c). By grep, the 1.7.0 build (sha256 1767dfc5…) has the same write classes, short path included; it was not read in full. Seed keys: none confines the short-mode command; extensions.shortdoi.autoretrieve=long (shipped default) keeps the on-add path off short mode.
- § 2026-10-04 by-itself: fail‡ → pass‡ — counted: an item notifier runs the autoretrieve mode on every added item, from any writer, sync downloads included. Shipped long lower-cases the DOI, expands a shortDOI to its long DOI from the handle record's alias, fills an empty DOI from a single Crossref hit, removes only its three pref-named tags, and adds a link attachment on a multiple hit; mode short would replace a long DOI with its shortDOI alias. The handler acts only on add events and returns when autoretrieve is unset or none, a shipped option. The short, long and check menu commands act on the selection, so the short-mode command that decided the first pass is the person's own edit. Setting: extensions.shortdoi.autoretrieve=none. Shipped long would also pass if expanding a shortDOI is not a rewrite; that is unruled, so none is named. Correction to the ‡ line above: long mode does more than lower-case, it replaces a shortDOI with its long DOI. This is README's declared automatic-mode key; doctor.py:278 @ 3780fb7 tests for True, which a string preference never matches. The 1.7.0 build has the same gate (bootstrap.js:70-75, gate 73 @ asset sha256 1767dfc5…). Re-judgement and verifier agree (zoteroshortdoi.js:28-32, 53-66 (gate 60), 74-78; lib/menus.js:19, 28-30, 42, 58-72; lib/doi-updater.js:66-70, 247-259, 286-311; lib/doi-service.js:104-117; prefs.js:1 @ v1.6.0 asset sha256 a52a846d…, tag object 23fd543 → 2ec547c).
- ∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling: pass‡§ → fail — (1): in every mode, an item with an empty DOI goes to a Crossref OpenURL query built from its title, journal, volume, issue, first author and date, and a single hit is written into DOI with no identifier check (§7 item 44). Triggers: the add notifier in short, long or check mode, and the item-menu Short, Long and Check commands on the selection, which read no preference. Secondary: short mode replaces a long DOI with its shortDOI and keeps the long one nowhere (2); long and check write the handle of cleanDOI's first match and drop any other text in the field (2). Setting: none; extensions.shortdoi.autoretrieve=none removes only the add trigger. Verifier correction to the 1.7.0 note: in both 1.7.0 builds' check mode a resolved Crossref hit is only tagged, not written; short and long still write it. Re-judgement and verifier agree (zoteroshortdoi.js:53-66, 74-78; lib/menus.js:58-72; lib/doi-updater.js:196-201, 231-280, 286-311; lib/doi-service.js:51-64, 104-131 @ v1.6.0 asset sha256 a52a846d…; zotero/utilities openurl.js:35-117 @ 1dd38e2; 1.7.0 builds lib/doi-updater.js:192, 272-290)

It fails (1) through the Crossref OpenURL fill of an empty DOI and (2) through the cleanDOI and short-mode replacements, and it returns when autoretrieve is unset or none. The Short, Long and Check entries sit on main/library/item and pass getSelectedItems with no fallback, so they are not judged; the Tools submenu and the options pane only set preferences; there is no timer, sweep, remote route or toolkit bundle. The multiple-hit link attachment is a new child of the selected item, within its own attachment records. Setting: extensions.shortdoi.autoretrieve=none, which leaves the menu commands writing. - **Writes.**
  - DOI. Retrieve writes it only when empty (doiManager.js:96-108). Update replaces it only after the old DOI fails verification (:190-236).

- ‡ 2026-10-03 normalisation: pass → fail — the menu Update command replaces another writer's DOI with a newly retrieved one. Verification is a Crossref /works lookup plus a 0.86 title-similarity gate. So a valid DataCite DOI, such as an arXiv 10.48550 or Zenodo DOI, fails verification and is replaced with a Crossref title match, and so is a valid DOI whose title differs. The prior-DOI Extra line keeps the old value, but the field is still rewritten. The old DOI is read at :194 and overwritten at :230 with network awaits in between and no re-check. Retrieve fills only an empty DOI, and tag removal touches only its own four tags. The re-judgement returned pass on the drafts' backup carve-out; the verifier refuted it, and its fail is used. It runs from the menu only, so an automatic-only reading would pass it (chrome/content/doiManager.js:19, 96-108, 194, 202-218, 229-232, 656-668, 702-713, 721-736; bootstrap.js:115-143 @ v1.1.6 asset sha256 5d6fffd0…, tag a3a3eca). Seed keys: none.
- § 2026-10-04 by-itself: fail‡ → pass‡ — counted: none. Startup loads doiManager.js, whose init only records its config, then injects FTL and registers an item-context submenu; the only timer positions the menu. There is no notifier, timer or handler. Retrieve, Update and Validate each start from getSelectedItems, so Update's DOI replacement, which decided the first pass, is the person's own edit. Setting: none needed. Re-judgement and verifier agree (bootstrap.js:20-41, 110-143, 146-153; chrome/content/doiManager.js:48-55, 66-68, 166-168, 561-563 @ v1.1.6 asset sha256 5d6fffd0…, tag a3a3eca).
- ∆ 2026-10-04 third ruling: pass‡§ → fail — (1): Retrieve fills an empty DOI from a Crossref OpenURL query built from the item's metadata or, failing that, a Crossref REST title and author search accepted at a title similarity of 0.86 or more. Update replaces an existing DOI that fails the same title-similarity check with a hit from the same search. Neither step checks an identifier. Trigger: the item-context submenu on the selection. Setting: none; the add-on reads no preferences. Re-judgement and verifier agree (chrome/content/doiManager.js:19-25, 66-121, 166-245, 256-332, 339-430, 640-654; bootstrap.js:110-143 @ v1.1.6 asset sha256 5d6fffd0…, tag a3a3eca)

Retrieve, Update and Validate each read getSelectedItems and alert when nothing is selected, with no collection or library fallback; getRegularItems only filters by type. There is no notifier, interval, endpoint or toolkit bundle, and it reads no preference. **11. ZotMeta 2.1.**

- **Pin.** Release v2.1 asset zotmeta-2.1.xpi, sha256 2a20b823119cd3348afe03ed1d0ad099d2f6ab8d8d6f48389fdf03ef506fc99e. HEAD adc1bd7ce8aeb85ee70e3c561362ff751be57d66 (2026-10-01). Licence MIT.
- **A1 ranges.** src/manifest.json:11-12 @adc1bd7 and shipped: 7.0 to 10.0.*. §38 recorded 7.0 to 9.0.* (import-sourcing.md:5409).
- **Writes.**
  - Field overwrites per its map (journal.js:143-155; book.js:138-145; arxiv.js:248-258).
  - PMID and PMCID Extra lines (pubmed.js:209-235; journal.js:156).
  - Identifier fields (utilities.js:598-610).
  - Two type-0 status tags. The failure tag is written only when tagFailedItems is on, which is off by default (prefs.js:3; utilities.js:5-6, 455-495, 462).
  - New parents for orphan PDFs (zotmeta.js:480-505).
  - Prefs. Its SQL is SELECT only (preferences.js:281-360).
- **A3 fail on a corrected basis.** The verifier changed the basis without flagging a refutation. applyMetadata overwrites title, creators and the other mapped fields with no backup and no conflict check (utilities.js:420-435). The desk's PMID/PMCID rationale does not hold:
  - The default source order tries the DOI source first (utilities.js:396-401). getMetaData returns the first non-empty source (journal.js:77-83), and the DOI payload has no PMID or PMCID (journal.js:123-133).
