## 7. Open questions the workers raised

Each item names the families that raised it.

### Questions whose answers change §3 results

1. **A3 scope** (identity-hygiene verifier; search-new desk). Does A3 judge automatic paths only, or all shipped behaviour, including user-invoked commands? The desk applied both readings: BBT passed on automatic paths, while Zutilo failed on user-invoked commands. Resolved 2026-10-03: see Standards as applied. Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling. Re-ruled 2026-10-04 by the third ruling (trigger-neutral).

   - On the all-paths reading, BBT, Zutilo, Zoplicate and arXiv Workflow fail.
   - On an automatic-only reading, BBT, Zutilo and arXiv Workflow pass. Zoplicate fails nothing on this profile, because action=cancel and its only automatic item write (the \_saveData re-parenting) moves children rather than destroying them.
   - Also open: does "destroy" cover user-confirmed recoverable trash (Duplicate Cleaner, FLoRA ban) and user-chosen permanent erase (Watch Folder)? Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling. (superseded 2026-10-04, third ruling)

2. **Configuration-gated results** (counts and notes-ui verifiers). One rule is needed for all of these:

   - Opt-in paths that destroy data: open-citations writeExtra, Zotero MCP write.enabled, openalex correctArxivArticles. Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling.
   - Service that exists only through configuration: Actions and Tags' A2 rests on a script rule production does not have.
   - Production prefs versus shipped defaults: Attachment Scanner fails on production prefs and would pass on shipped defaults. Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling.

3. **BBT consolidation clause** (identity-hygiene). On Zotero 10.0.5, no merge path on this profile records a loser's citekey as an alias:

   - Zotero.Items.merge is a deprecated shim over mergeItems.mjs (items.js:1037-1041 @ ca61760).
   - The native merge pane calls mergeItems.mjs directly (duplicatesMergePane.js:34, 203-211), exactly as Zoplicate does.
   - BBT's shim is gated on extraMerge\*, which ship false and are unset here (defaults.js:54-56).
   - What every merge does leave is dc:replaces (mergeItems.mjs:426-477), which lifecycle.py:33-83 reads.

   The options are to restate A3's clause against dc:replaces plus lifecycle detection, or to require extraMergeCitekeys=true as a doctor row, accepting that only deprecated-API callers honour it. Resolved 2026-10-03: see Standards as applied. Related:

   - Spec §15 item 03 (assembly-design.md:521) treats the hazard as specific to Zoplicate; it is wider.
   - Not a hazard, but previously unrecorded: the native pane clones the master for display (duplicatesMergePane.js:195), BBT blanks a clone's key (better-bibtex.ts:89-93), and toJSON omits the blank, so the master's key survives a native merge.

4. **A2 criterion** (counts verifier; search-new desk).

   - Does an in-Zotero column serve scoping-review? The desk failed scite and passed the count plugins on the same basis.
   - Every Extra writer is credited with capture because capture carries Extra verbatim (CONTEXT.md:57). That is true of any writer.
   - Step-map scope: does search cover Zotero-side retrieval (ZotSeek)? Does scoping-review cover appraisal (PubPeer, FLoRA) and reading status (Reading List)? Does daily-log cover a log the vault never reads (Reading Time)? Does protecting capture without serving a step meet A2 (Duplicate Cleaner)? Is Scholar Sidekick search or capture?

### Facts for the spec

05. **The auto-update premise is refuted** (identity-hygiene verifier).
    - prefs.js:46 sets extensions.update.autoUpdateDefault=false, and every candidate's applyBackgroundUpdates=1 follows it.
    - No xpi in extensions/ is newer than Sep 3, although BBT 9.0.64 shipped Sep 9.
    - Newer builds move only by hand: BBT 9.0.68, arXiv Workflow 0.4.0, Linter 4.0.1, Zotero OCR 0.9.6, DOI Fix 1.1.9. None has been observed loaded.
    - A1 observations are version-bound. Zoplicate's 10.0.\* cap would appDisable it on Zotero 10.1.
06. **The cap bypass is evidence, not inference** (counts verifier).
    - In the 10.0.5 omni.ja, AddonUpdateChecker.sys.mjs:189-198 and XPIDatabase.sys.mjs:723-748 raise a stored range to '\*' when an update entry lacks strict_max_version.
    - That is why scite, Zotero TLDR and KeepZotero load despite their shipped caps (the assembly spec §14 catalogue-staleness row).
    - It also means §38's cap-based exclusions do not settle loadability (§5).
07. **Which channel carries identifiers** (identifiers-metadata).
    - SNAPSHOT_FIELDS (literature_notes.py:34-52) carries DOI, extra and tags, but not PMID or PMCID.
    - Schema 44 makes PMID and PMCID native on journalArticle, and both the PMCID fetcher and Cita write them there.
    - events.py:258 tests lowercase pmid, while Zotero's JSON key is PMID.
08. **Shared Extra keys and colliding grammars** (identifiers-metadata, counts-summaries).
    - PMID and PMCID have three writers: the PMCID fetcher (appends), ZotMeta (canonicalises in place and blanks duplicates) and Cita (clears and re-appends, or moves to the native field).
    - DOI lines have one writer, DOI Fix, and that path is unreachable on 10.
    - No key is owned by one plugin.
    - The count plugins citation-tally, tangzhao20, getcitation and open-citations (with writeExtra on) strip or mangle each other's lines. arxiv-marker strips tangzhao20's line. gscc and open-citations leave each other alone.
    - Declaring more than one count plugin is a hazard of its own.
09. **Writers racing on add** (identifiers-metadata).
    - Writers on the same add: PMCID fetcher (add and modify, writing back a stale whole Extra), DOI Manager (add), Linter (add, priority 666), openalex (startup sweep), reginalluna (monthly sweep). Only Linter's priority orders them.
    - Linter's correct-doi-long expands short DOIs on add (prefs.js:62; linter.js:6350-6362), while DOI Manager's short mode converts long DOIs to short. The two would fight.
10. **One mechanism behind merge-time Extra rewrites** (identity-hygiene, identifiers-metadata, search-new).
    - Zoplicate (mergeItems.ts:48), arXiv Workflow (arxiv-merge.ts:218) and the native merge pane (duplicatesMergePane.js:209) all call fromJSON non-strict, which migrates Extra lines into fields (item.js:5656-5697). That is the class of §14's local-API PATCH row (assembly-design.md:493). One live leg closes all three.
    - Whole-item replace or restore can set the native citationKey outside BBT: arxiv-marker undo, Metadata Hunter's translator items, Duplicate Cleaner's fill, FLoRA's copy, Zutilo's pasteJSON. A second leg reads BBT 9.0.63's reaction.
11. **DOI Manager id ambiguity** (identifiers-metadata).
    - README.md:125 and research_vault/templates/zotero-addons.md:5 declare zoteroshortdoi@wiernik.org. The desk cited README.md:16; the verifier corrected it to :125.
    - Three lines ship under that id with different write behaviour: Julius 1.7.0, reginalluna 2.2.0 (with a default-on monthly sweep) and dahliasan 1.7.0.
    - The installed 1.6.0 neither loads on 10.0.5 nor self-updates.
    - Doctor checks id plus appDisabled, which cannot tell the three lines apart.
12. **A BBT auto-export on a CLI-owned path name** (identity-hygiene).
    - An enabled library-wide Better CSL JSON auto-export targets kh-vault/system/bibliography.json (prefs.js:133; last written 2026-10-03 17:42).
    - research_vault/bibliography.py:3-5 and :15 own that relative path and regenerate it, with no auto-export.
    - kh-vault lacks wiki/, literature/ and .raw/ (measured), so it is not a user vault by AGENTS.md's definition.
    - Nothing checks for an auto-export targeting a vault. doctor.py:230-240 probes only BBT's git pref.
    - doctor.py:232 @ 3780fb7 treats an absent git pref as off, but BBT 9.0.63 ships git=config (defaults.js:64). Where the pref is absent, doctor would report git=off while the effective mode pushes from any export target that opts in (auto-export.ts:194-254). kh-vault has no opt-in.
13. **Canonicalising tags at rest** (identity-hygiene).
    - Zotero's native rename (tags.js:283-364 @ ca61760) does it in one UPDATE OR REPLACE that sets type=0 (:314), so collisions merge silently and automatic tags become manual.
    - The old tag row is purged (:347), and the old name survives only in the transient notifier payload (:330-345).
    - No undo is staged. UndoHistory is in memory only and capped at 100 (undoHistory.js:27, 48).
    - Items are marked synced=0 (:320-324), so the change reaches zotero.org (§14 sync row, assembly-design.md:494).
    - assembly-design.md:289 says automatic tags regrow on refresh; that does not address the above. Zutilo has no rename and no single-tag removal.
    - What survives of the original form: the literature note's tags snapshot until the next capture (CONTEXT.md:57); zotero.sqlite.bak (2026-10-03 16:19) and zotero.sqlite.1.bak (2026-10-02) in D:\\Zotero until they are overwritten; and any export taken beforehand.
14. **Spec lines the evidence dates or contradicts** (identity-hygiene, attachments, notes-ui).
    - assembly-design.md §14 rows 484 and 487 say Attachment Scanner uses an emoji preset (cited as prefs.js:32-35) and has two tag generations. Production has only the hash preset.
    - §7.0, line 289, credits Zutilo with bulk tag operations.
    - §15 item 03, line 521, frames the merge hazard as specific to Zoplicate.
    - Lines 496 and 557 say a MarkDB run would strip the 3 ObsCite tags today. At 825ef2d, an invalid sourcedir returns early and zero records make a dry run, and the tags are still present while MarkDB is active. The armed hazard is a partly readable vault.
    - Sequencing for MarkDB: production yamlkeyword is citekey (prefs.js:91), while literature notes carry citationKey (literature_notes.py:57). So removetags=addonly must be set before or together with the yamlkeyword fix.
15. **Zotero child notes land in literature-note bodies** (attachments-fulltext-ocr).
    - Capture renders Zotero child notes (capture.py:206; literature_notes.py:443).
    - Notes that would land there: OCR text notes, Mktero snapshot notes, Zotadata download notes, TL;DR notes, Cita's JSON Citations notes and Notero's sync-JSON notes, unless the lane filters them. §35 already flagged the OCR note.
    - An OCR twin gives its parent two PDF children. With production's monitor on, that triggers Attachment Scanner's duplicate tag (attachmentscanner.js:430-443, 471-474), and capture carries the tag. Mktero's two JSON children trigger it too.
16. **Tag counts disagree** (attachments and notes-ui verifiers).
    - /tags numItems reports ObsCite 4, triage:needed 50, #nosource 445 and #nonfile 10.
    - Item queries give 3, 49, 175 and 2, with or without includeTrashed.
    - numItems is a raw itemTags count (tags.js:243-246). The cause of the gap is open.
17. **#135** (notes-ui).
    - Actions and Tags 2.6.1 can be configured for both actions without a build: a customScript rule shown in the item menu (actions.ts:43-65, 266-308; menu.ts:39-42, 297-305, 325-352).
    - Opening a note uses Zotero.launchURL on an obsidian:// URL for literature/<citationKey>. The vault path is built at literature_notes.py:158, not :160; the issue says literatures/<key>.md. launchURL hands non-HTTP schemes to the OS handler (zotero.js:1231-1262 @ ca61760).
    - Adding uses Zotero.Utilities.Internal.exec on wsl.exe with the capture verb (utilities_internal.js:670-702; capture.py:445; __main__.py:785-788). pyproject defines no console script.
    - Both need a live check.
    - Re-enabling the add-on re-arms rule hara0, which tags every new item triage:needed. Nothing in research-vault gives that tag a meaning, and 49 items already carry it, plus 73 /unread tags.
    - Zutilo can do neither action. MarkDB-Connect already ships the open half, given sourcedir, yamlkeyword=citationKey and removetags=addonly.
18. **Add-on Market's execJS** (notes-ui). It runs remote JavaScript signed by the author (registerScheme.ts:146-170; shipped zoteroaddons.js:9091). Should a vault declare or reject an add-on whose write list includes that? Resolved 2026-10-04 by the second ruling. For A3 only: the route is remote and counted (row 42); whether a vault declares such an add-on is the spec's question.
19. **Licences** (all families).
    - Conflicts at the pin: Chartero (Apache-2.0 vs AGPL-2.0-or-later), Zotadata (MIT vs AGPL-3.0-or-later), zotero-openalex (GPL-3.0 vs AGPL-3.0-or-later), BBT (MIT vs ISC; already in §28).
    - No licence recorded: PMCID fetcher, scite, Open PDF, getcitation, Review Assistant, PubPeer, Folder Import, Reading Time.
    - Claimed only: RapidOCR (MPL-2.0) and ZotSeek (MIT).
    - Zutilo's LICENSE.md says MPL-2.0 while GitHub reports NOASSERTION.
    - Zotero core and zotero-schema: the identity-hygiene verifier recorded no licence at ca61760 and 70c3aa9. The notes-ui desk names AGPL-3.0 for zotero/zotero@ca61760.
    - Nothing from an unlicensed body is quoted here.
20. **Pins and drift** (all families).
    - BBT HEAD 71cec80 is one test-only commit past the v9.0.68 tag, 1af0bfe.
    - DOI Manager v1.6.0 is annotated tag 23fd543, which points to commit 2ec547c.
    - Zotero ca61760 was checked against the installed omni.ja. mergeItems.mjs is byte-identical; item.js, items.js, duplicatesMergePane.js, tags.js and undoHistory.js differ only in formatting.
    - Schema 44 in the omni.ja is byte-identical to zotero-schema 70c3aa9.
    - Zoplicate's HEAD carries an unreleased cross-type merge that writes a child note (6d94dc1).
    - Some A3 and A4 reads are at repo HEAD while the installed or released version differs: Add-on Market 10.0.0 versus V10.0.1, and MCP v1.6.0, which lacks trash_item. Re-read them against the version actually installed when a live leg runs. (At 2026-10-03; superseded for MCP's A3: the second ruling and check remote-write-routes re-read v1.6.0's write gate, 2026-10-04, §4 row 44.)
21. **Data leaving the machine** (counts, attachments, search-new).
    - scite sends every library DOI to api.scite.ai at startup and daily.
    - Zotero TLDR sends every unmapped title to semanticscholar.org at each startup, although its map has stayed empty.
    - PubPeer POSTs DOIs on every add or modify.
    - Open PDF's click-gated upload includes an RDF export of the selected items.

### Facts from the live run (2026-10-04)

Each item names its method and raw log. Paths are under /tmp/lane23-live/zotero/ unless an item names the Obsidian leg, whose paths are under /tmp/lane23-live/obsidian/.

22. **The test instance** (probe and file reads).
    - Zotero 10.0.5, schema 44, BBT 9.0.68 (xpi sha256 d3699a34…), profile ~/.zotero/zotero/rfnse7tz.default, data directory ~/Zotero (logs/00-probe-initial.txt; logs/10-a1-run.txt; logs/12-a1b-run.txt). Those versions dated from the evening of 2026-10-03, after an upgrade from Zotero 10.0.2 with BBT 9.0.64 (§6.4 Setup gives the method).
    - Production runs BBT 9.0.63, so every ◊ result was observed beside a newer BBT than §4's pins. Like item 5's, these observations are version-bound.
    - It did not sync. libraries.lastSync stayed 2026-06-29T16:30:21-05:00 in the snapshot and after restarts, with 2 items synced=0, although the profile sets sync.server.username and autoSync defaults to true. The log shows Zotero reading the sync password and then skipping auto-sync because sync is not enabled (logs/03-snapshot-db-state.txt; step2/tldr/zotero.out:890-892). logins.json was not read; the permission system denied it.
23. **Sideload route** (file install). Copying a shipped xpi into the profile's extensions/ directory as <id>.xpi, with user.js setting extensions.autoDisableScopes=0 and extensions.startupScanScopes=1, installs and enables it at the next start. extensions.json then records foreignInstall=true, location app-profile and no sourceURI (a1/user.js; logs/10-a1-run.txt; a1/a1-extensions-table.tsv). On that start the add-on background update check did not run (its timer pref is unchanged in a1/prefs.after.js:12), so A1 was read before any compatibility raise.
24. **Forcing the add-on update check.** user.js app.update.lastUpdateTime.addon-background-update-timer=1 makes the check run at startup; in A1b it fired at 01:54:53 (pref value 1791096893; logs/12-a1b-run.txt).
    - Zotero TLDR: the check raised the stored range from 6.999–7.0.\* to 6.999–\* and Zotero enabled the add-on at runtime, startup reason ADDON_ENABLE (step2/tldr/zotero-run1.out:1182, 1196; step2/tldr/extensions.after-run1.json). This is item 6's mechanism observed, on the test instance. It does not show when production's raises happened; row 18's "still inferred" is about production.
    - Scholar Sidekick, getcitation and Review Assistant, whose update entries carry strict_max_version 9.\* or 9.999.\*, and Zotadata, whose update URL returns 404: nothing changed (a1b/extensions.after.json; a1b/update-manifests.txt).
25. **The connector moves field-shaped Extra lines** (BBT-only control run). /connector/saveItems moved the fixtures' Citation Key:, PMID: and PMCID: Extra lines into the native citationKey, PMID and PMCID fields, so an item saved this way keeps only Extra lines that are not fields, such as count lines (step2/control/dbdiff.txt). The Zoplicate and arXiv Workflow legs therefore carried no field-shaped Extra line into a merge, and item 10's merge-time migration was not exercised.
26. **A1 ran as a batch.** All 27 add-ons ran together in one profile for 60 s. That log holds 13 'uncaught exception: undefined' lines against 1 in a BBT-only start of the restored instance (logs/11-a1-restore.txt), so add-ons could have interacted; each A1 reading (appDisabled and the startup call) is per add-on. Within the 60 s, add-ons sent the test library's data out: pubpeer.com and api.openalex.org answered HTTP 429, and DOI Manager 2.2.0's first-startup Crossref sweep sent 5 requests (§4 rows 7, 17 and 48; item 21).
27. **Debug logging.** Starting Zotero with -ZoteroDebugText sends add-on errors to the log, and Zotero then writes browser.dom.window.dump.enabled=true into prefs.js. Every leg's profile was discarded, and the final start used the plain command (logs/30-step3-final-restore.txt).
28. **Probe timing.** One local-API request reached /api/ before Zotero finished initialising, before the zotero-loaded event. Zotero logged a creator-not-found error while loading items, then held every API call waiting for library 1's items (step2/metadata-hunter/zotero-invalid-run.out:311). That run was invalidated and re-run as metadata-hunter-r2; the start helper then waited for the zotero-loaded log line before probing (step2/metadata-hunter/run.log; step2/metadata-hunter-r2/run.log).
29. **dateAdded in a Zotero 10.0.5 merge.** In Zoplicate's keep merge, which calls mergeItems.mjs directly, the master's dateAdded stayed 07:08:52 while the loser's was 07:08:44 (step2/zoplicate-keep/dbdiff.txt). That contradicts the earliest-dateAdded effect §4 records for mergeItems.mjs in rows 2 and 46. Zotero's own merge pane was not run, so whether its path differs is unobserved.
30. **SQLite side files.** By the leg's reading, Zotero holds its database in exclusive locking mode and writes no zotero.sqlite-shm; the Step 0 data directory had none (logs/02-step0-snapshot.txt). The leg's read-only Python sqlite3 opens of the snapshot database created one, which every restore copied into ~/Zotero (32,768 bytes beside a 0-byte WAL) until it was removed before the final start (logs/30-step3-final-restore.txt). The leg verifier's method, a read-only open with immutable=1, creates none.
31. **Watch Folder and the recognizer.** In mode1, Watch Folder sent each watched PDF to Zotero's recognizer at services.zotero.org (step2/watch-folder/zotero.out:1761, 1765, 2208, 2212). The fixture PDFs were synthetic (fixtures/mkpdf.py), so no third-party content was uploaded. This is data leaving the machine, as in item 21.
32. **Production Zotero 10.0.5 and BBT 9.0.63** (the Obsidian leg's read-only requests to production; obsidian logs/step3-curl.log and logs/step3-curl/).
    - Zotero 10.0.5's server cancels, with an empty reply, a request whose User-Agent starts with Mozilla/ or that carries an Origin header, unless it sends zotero-allowed-request or x-zotero-connector-api-version or the endpoint opts in; narrow exemptions cover /test/ paths and a navigation request to /connector/ping (Zotero server.js:411-427 @ e60dc9a; local copy obsidian downloads/zotero-e60dc9a/server.js). Measured on production through BBT's endpoints: an added Origin header, and Electron's default User-Agent, were both cancelled; a request with neither was answered.
    - BBT's json-rpc.ts and pull-export.ts at v9.0.63, v9.0.65 and v9.0.68 contain no allowRequestsFromUnsafeWebContent opt-in (code read; obsidian logs/step3-bbt-later-versions.log). So BBT's JSON-RPC answers a client that sends neither header, such as curl, and cancels a browser-shaped one.
    - BBT 9.0.63's item.search on 10.0.5: the string-term form fails with an invalid-condition error on blockStart (v9.0.65 switched to groupStart; code read, unmeasured); conditions carrying a fourth 'required' element fail as no longer supported, and v9.0.65 and v9.0.68 still pass caller terms through; array conditions without that element work. research-vault calls only api.ready, item.attachments and item.export (§4 row 1). item.export (CSL JSON) answered 200 at 01:53.
    - Production has one library, My Library (id 1), with no groups and 48 top-level collections; the CAYW probe answers ready; the local API is enabled in prefs.js.
33. **Production's auto-exports** (the Obsidian leg's read-only read of production prefs.js, sha256 d546a541…; obsidian logs/step3-prod-prefs.log). Two BBT auto-exports are enabled. One exports library 1 as Better CSL JSON to `\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\home\eranr\kh-vault\system\bibliography.json`, last updated 2026-10-03T17:42:41-05:00; that is item 12's, and its path, system/bibliography.json, is on §2a's machine-surface roster. The other exports collection 57 as Better BibLaTeX to `C:\Users\<user>\memoria-private\.memoria\memoria.bib`, the second prefs.js:133-134 entry, which row 1 and item 12 leave unnamed. The Obsidian leg's verifier was not permitted to read the production profile, so this item is leg-reported, not verified.
34. **Test-instance state after the run.**
    - Final restore at 02:46 from the Step 0 snapshot, which leaves no user.js. The stray zotero.sqlite-shm was removed (item 30). Zotero was started with the plain command (pid 2746068), and the probe answered Zotero 10.0.5 with BBT 9.0.68. extensions.json lists only better-bibtex@iris-advies.com 9.0.68, active and not appDisabled; its sha256 0a6fa2b3… and prefs.js's f4d41fcb… equal the snapshot's (logs/30-step3-final-restore.txt).
    - Ordering: the full-tree compare against snapshot.manifest.sha256 ran at 02:46:04, before the stray -shm was removed at 02:46:33, and the manifest itself lists that -shm. After the removal the tree is the Step 0 file set, which had no -shm.
    - The leg expected the WAL to be checkpointed at the next shutdown. The leg verifier found it checkpointed while Zotero ran, at 02:51:32. Re-read at 03:21:18 with Zotero still running as pid 2746068: zotero.sqlite is sha256 982b10f9…, not the snapshot's 118a7fdf…, both it and the 0-byte WAL carry mtime 02:51:32, and there is no -shm (/tmp/lane23-live/fold-A/restore-check.txt). The leg verifier's read-only diff against the snapshot database finds changes only in version.lastcheck and version.repository; libraries is unchanged.
    - Kept on disk: the snapshot copy (/tmp/lane23-live/zotero/snapshot, about 2.7 GB) and the scratch watch folder (/tmp/lane23-live/zotero/watch). Every post-leg data directory and profile was discarded. (At 2026-10-04 03:21; superseded for the snapshot copy: at 03:58 it was no longer under /tmp/lane23-live/zotero/, and ~/rv-lanes23-raw-logs-2026-10-04.tar.gz does not contain it; the watch folder is in both (ls and tar listing, 2026-10-04).)
    - The Zotero leg did not contact or touch production Zotero (localhost:23119 and its profile). Production's only contacts were the Obsidian leg's read-only requests and its read of prefs.js (items 32 and 33).
    - Incidents: at Step 0, pgrep -f matched the agent's own bash wrapper, which received SIGTERM along with Zotero; Zotero still exited cleanly (logs/01-step0-stop.txt). Stopping a backgrounded helper also stopped the Zotero it had started (the invalid metadata-hunter run).

### Facts and questions from the verified checks (2026-10-04)

Three checks re-read the cells they list on 2026-10-04, each a check then an adversarial verifier whose result is used. They changed no §3 result. Each listed row has a §4 line beginning "¤ 2026-10-04 check". Items 35-37 are their findings, dated 2026-10-04; items 38-43 are the questions they raised.

35. **semantic-zotero: the A1 batch installed the real release, and Zotero loaded it** (check semantic-zotero; raw-log reads, xpi re-hashes and GitHub API reads, 2026-10-04).
    - Row 25's A1 rests on AgiNetz/semantic-zotero's own v0.3.0 asset, the repository and id §38 recorded, with one sha256 across download, installed copy, §4 Pin and GitHub digest, and with its startup run to its last line (§4 row 25, ¤ line).
    - Every other A1 pass◊ of the batch (rows 7, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15, 17, 21, 22, 23, 32, 33, 34, 44, 45, 46, 48, 49, 50, 51, 52 and 54) and row 19's supplementary leg: the shipped manifest id equals §3's, the shipped and stored versions equal the pin, the sha256 agrees across download, installed copy, §4 and the GitHub digest (row 19's release has no digest, so its asset was downloaded again to /tmp and re-hashed), every source repository is a non-fork, and the tag commit matches where §3 names one (rows 7, 45, 50 and 52). The only version difference in the batch is row 47's, which §3 already records.
    - §4 row 25's Pin said "the Zotero 7–10 port landed 2026-09-30", the date the port was authored; it was merged on 2026-10-03, and the Pin now says so.
    - import-sourcing.md:5378 and :5415 describe semantic-zotero only as it was at aa7ad52, a dead Zotero 6 overlay. That was accurate at their 2026-09-04 read; the record carries no note that the v0.3.0 bootstrap release superseded it on 2026-10-03.
    - Raw-log bookkeeping, no cell: a1/a1-results.json flags rows 44-54 as having no sha in the evidence. §4 now carries their shas, and all of them match the downloads.
36. **remote-write-routes: rows 1 and 44 are read the same way** (check remote-write-routes; code reads at the pins, production omni.ja files under C:/Program Files/Zotero read with python zipfile, a reg.exe query, 2026-10-04; not live-tested).
    - Both register a remote route at startup with no preference check, and a local client can use either to change an existing citation key: BBT's JSON-RPC item.regenerate_key, and MCP's write_metadata, whose write_tag set can also strip tags another writer set. They differ only through the settings clause: MCP's own write.enabled turns its writes off and ships off, while no BBT preference gates the endpoint (§4 rows 1 and 44, ¤ lines).
    - The other endpoint rows read the same way: row 42's execJS fails on the same discriminator as row 1; rows 35, 49 and 52 write nothing in (a) or (b); and Notero (row 43) registers zotero://notero, whose only handler stores a Notion auth token. A sweep of all 54 row bundles found these registrations: Zotero.Server.Endpoints in rows 1, 35, 49 and 52; own zotero:// routes in rows 37 (note), 42 and 43; and a socket server in row 44. Row 34's WebSocket hits are an outbound transcription client inside a bundled SDK, not an endpoint. No bundle registers a command-line handler.
    - The zotero-plugin-toolkit route zotero://plugin?action=install is registered by the shipped bundles of 21 screened rows, 2, 4, 9, 12, 16, 19, 20, 29, 31, 33, 35, 36, 37, 38, 39, 42, 44, 45, 50, 52 and 54, plus the excluded Better Authors; each bundle was matched to its table pin by manifest id and version. The route lives on the shared `Zotero._toolkitGlobal`, which BasicTool's constructor creates and createZToolkit runs at startup, so it is registered once for all of them. Its doAction calls AddonManager.getInstallForURL and then install(), with no confirm and no preference read.
    - On production the route is reachable from outside Zotero. HKCR\\zotero\\shell\\open\\command is "C:\\Program Files\\Zotero\\zotero.exe" -url "%1". In Zotero 10.0.5's app/omni.ja, commandLineHandler.js:36-44 passes a zotero: URL to ZoteroPane.loadURI, and zoteroPane.js:5015-5022 calls a no-content extension's doAction directly; getExtension matches by prefix (ZoteroProtocolHandler.mjs:922-935). xpinstall.signatures.required appears in defaults/preferences/zotero.js as true at :25 and false at :1483, the later line being the effective one; greprefs.js:1173 is false, and production prefs.js does not set it. So an add-on installed this way need not be signed.
    - The toolkit's debug bridge, zotero://ztoolkit-debug, keeps its confirm on production in every bundle. It asks unless the caller or production supplies a password; production prefs.js has no extensions.zotero.debug-bridge.password, so a supplied one fails; no bundle sets the bypass true; and Better Notes ties the bypass to its build env, hard-coded production (BetterNotes.js:47854-47859).
    - Row 44's § line counts collection edits as hits, while row 1's places collection membership outside (a). No result turns on it, because write.enabled gates both in row 44.
    - Re-read trail, session-local and not archived: the check's bundle sweeps /tmp/rwr-scan.py and /tmp/rwr-scan2.py, with reads under /tmp/rwr-omni and /tmp/rwr-x; the verifier's sweeps /tmp/rwv-scan.py, /tmp/rwv-scan2.py and /tmp/rwv-scan3.py.
37. **scanner-vs-lifecycle: a trashed child attachment makes capture refuse a note** (check scanner-vs-lifecycle; code at worktree HEAD 8cedd18, an offline replay, and read-only GETs to production at localhost:23119, 2026-10-04).
    - A captured note whose provenance tuple lists a child attachment that is later trashed is classified trashed by lifecycle and refused by capture until the child is restored or purged. verify reports it on each run and keeps one review-queue entry open, and a concurrent re-key reads as trashed, which propagate does not map. Under production prefs, Attachment Scanner's notifier triggers that state by itself (§4 row 26, ¤ line).
    - New measurement: lifecycle's trash read (/items/trash?format=versions) carries child attachment keys. On production all 19 trashed imported_url snapshots and all 8 trashed PubMed entries are in the trash map and none is in the items map; of the 19 snapshots, 15 carry their own deleted flag and 4 are in the trash route through a trashed parent. The evidence had recorded only that /children leaves trashed children out (§4 row 26).
    - The classify branch was already recorded under row 4 (trashUnannotatedPDF, a command the person runs); new are the by-itself trigger and the measurement.
    - Not observed on a real captured note: the only literature/ directory found under ~ was the empty scratch vault's, ~/rv-lane3-scratch-vault/literature.
    - No standalone lint verb exists; lint_lifecycle is called by capture, verify and propagate.
    - Raw logs: /tmp/lane23-live/scanner-vs-lifecycle/production-trash-read.txt, offline-replay.txt and verifier-reread.txt (re-read 2026-10-04T08:44:48Z). They postdate ~/rv-lanes23-raw-logs-2026-10-04.tar.gz and are not in it.
38. **Does §2a's capability reading reach the toolkit install route?** (check remote-write-routes; the answer changes §3 results). Row 42's execJS fails on §2a's open capability reading. If that reading reaches zotero://plugin?action=install (item 36), the 21 rows that bundle it turn on it, and row 44 would turn on that route rather than on its MCP tools. If it does not, the ground that separates the route from execJS is unwritten: §2a's toolkit-route bullet says the route writes no item data, which is equally true of execJS. One candidate ground the check names: execJS runs its payload in Add-on Market's own scope, while the route installs a separate component that is judged on its own.
39. **Can a host-application preference be a confining setting?** (check remote-write-routes; the answer could change row 1). §2a's settings clause says a component passes if "a setting" turns the counted behaviour off, and for a Zotero add-on "that setting is a preference"; it does not say the preference must be the add-on's own. BBT's only off-switch for /better-bibtex/json-rpc is Zotero's extensions.zotero.httpServer.enabled. Row 1's § line rules it out on two grounds: it is not the add-on's preference, and capture posts to that endpoint (research_vault/zotero.py:465). Unruled.
40. **A1 when Zotero calls startup and the add-on's own init then throws** (check semantic-zotero; the answer could change rows 13 and 19). The check applied a rule that an add-on Zotero lists without loading does not pass, which is stricter than §1's A1 test (appDisabled=false). The two disagree where Zotero calls bootstrap startup and the add-on's own init then throws: row 19 (Zotero TLDR: startup called, then a TypeError in its data-storage init, recorded under A2) and row 13 (zotero-ner: a non-fatal load error). No reading for that shape is recorded, and neither row was re-judged on the stricter wording.
41. **Spec §3.4 step 4 and a child under a live parent** (check scanner-vs-lifecycle; no §3 result turns on it). Is the rule that any tuple key in the trash makes the note trashed (import-redesign-design.md:195, 197; lifecycle.py:88-91; tests/test_lifecycle.py:117-135) intended for a child attachment whose parent stays live? It makes capture refuse the note and masks a re-key from propagate while the child is in the trash. It is research-vault's own design choice, and its cost falls there rather than in the add-on.
42. **Is a child attachment within A3(a)'s "Extra, tags, fields or child notes"?** (check scanner-vs-lifecycle). The 2026-10-03 re-judgement set attachment trashing aside, while row 26's § line counts it. Row 26 does not turn on the answer; any other component that by itself trashes a child attachment a tuple records would carry the same capture refusal.
43. **On production, does the notifier trash a Connector-saved snapshot before capture first reads /children?** (check scanner-vs-lifecycle). For a snapshot saved before its PDF, code settles it: no race is needed (§4 row 26, ¤ line). Open: whether a Connector save of a PDF and a snapshot together trashes the snapshot before capture's first read; that turns on Connector save code, which was not read. A test-instance leg would settle it: connector-save an item with a PDF and a snapshot, with monitor_attachments=true and remove_snapshot=true; capture before and after BBT's key fill; then modify the parent and run verify. PubMed entries are trashed on any parent modify, with no PDF condition, so a tuple would list one only if capture ran while the notifier was not registered.

### Facts and questions from the third normalisation (2026-10-04)

The third normalisation re-judged every A3 cell on 2026-10-04 (§2a, "Third ruling"), each family's re-judgement then an adversarial verifier whose result is used. Items 44-54 are facts it settled, each with its verifier's finding; item 55 points to the readings it left open. Code was read at the pins §4 records; every network read was read-only.

44. **DOI Manager's modes and sweep** (rows 6 and 7; verifier upheld). DOI Manager 2.2.0 has four autoretrieve modes, short, long, check and none, and ships long. The add notifier runs the mode on every item any writer adds, and returns only when the preference is unset or none. In short, long and check alike, an item whose DOI field is empty first goes to a Crossref OpenURL query built from its title, journal, volume, issue, first author and date: one hit is written into DOI, none adds the no-DOI tag, several add a link attachment and the multiple-DOI tag. With a DOI present, short asks shortdoi.org and writes the lower-cased shortDOI in its place; long asks the doi.org handle API and writes the lower-cased handle, or for a shortDOI the record's second value, which on the four shortDOIs read (10/f45thf, 10/aabbe, 10/bfx6, 10/cn4x) was the HS_ALIAS long DOI; check writes the handle lower-cased when it differs from the field, which also drops text cleanDOI's pattern does not match; a 400, a 404 or a handle-not-found code tags the item invalid. monthly_verify (shipped true, last_run shipped empty) is checked at init and on a 24-hour timer; when due it runs check mode silently over every top-level user-library item and stamps last_run, so its first run is at the first startup after install. No mode or setting keeps doi.org validation, lower-casing and short-to-long expansion while removing the Crossref fill from every trigger: the fill runs before any mode code, the item-menu Short, Long and Check commands read only the three tag-string preferences, and autoretrieve=none with monthly_verify=false removes only the add and sweep triggers. 1.6.0 is the same without the sweep. Verifier: upheld; live handle reads of 10/aabbe and 10/bfx6 on 2026-10-04 returned HS_ADMIN at position 0 and HS_ALIAS at position 1. Refinement: in both 1.7.0 builds' check mode a resolved Crossref hit is only tagged, not written; short and long still write it. Long mode reads the alias by position rather than by type, which held on every handle read (zoteroshortdoi.js:29-34, 70-80, 82-86, 88-114; lib/menus.js:8, 34-63; lib/doi-updater.js:41-48, 162-252, 254-307; lib/doi-service.js:35-101; lib/doi-http.js:13-17; prefs.js:1-3 @ v2.2.0 asset sha256 299c46a3…; lib/doi-updater.js:196-201, 286-311 @ v1.6.0 asset sha256 a52a846d…; 1.7.0 builds lib/doi-updater.js:272-290).
45. **Meta Refresh has no switch for its title fallback, but a seeded threshold acts as one** (row 12; verifier upheld, with additions). titleSimilarityThreshold (shipped 0.85) is the only control. A non-exact source's hit is accepted only at a similarity at or above it, and similarity never exceeds 1, so a seeded value above 1, such as "2", rejects every title hit and leaves Crossref, OpenAlex and Semantic Scholar by DOI and Semantic Scholar by arXiv ID. The preferences pane clamps a typed value to 0–1, so the value has to be seeded, and "1" still accepts an identical normalised title. Per-source switches cannot do the same, because Semantic Scholar, OpenAlex and Crossref each serve both an ID lookup and a title lookup, and Semantic Scholar is the only arXiv source. readConfig reads the threshold at the start of every refresh and preprint scan, the item-pane button included, but Fetch citation counts falls back to a Semantic Scholar or OpenAlex title search with no similarity gate, which only useS2=false and useOpenAlex=false remove. The arXiv ID it treats as exact is pattern-picked from the DOI, url, Extra, archive and archiveID fields, and the published-version DOI and venue come from Semantic Scholar's own record. Verifier: upheld. Additions: readConfig parses the value with no clamp, so a seeded "2" survives; and the exact Crossref-by-DOI path keeps only the first title string and ignores Crossref's separate subtitle, and strips abstract markup (Crossref's works record for 10.1145/3442188.3445922, read 2026-10-04) (prefs.js:7, 11-14; content/scripts/metarefresh.js:1617-1630, 1668-1690, 1758-1777, 1883, 1891, 2043-2057, 2153-2170, 2187-2262, 2481-2502 @ v0.5.0 asset sha256 b1684aff…).
46. **The PMCID fetcher's Extra race** (row 5; verifier upheld, one cite corrected). A job snapshots Extra when it starts and, whenever it saves, sets Extra whole from the snapshot plus its own appended lines. Before that it waits for up to three NCBI requests, each for a 400 ms slot in one shared queue, plus network time. Any Extra change another writer or the person saves in that window is lost; native fields and tags are not, because it fills only an empty PMID or PMCID field and only adds tags. The write-back runs on any identifier write and, with tags on, on any fetch that returns a MeSH heading or keyword, so on an item with a DOI but no PMID or PMCID every modify by any writer starts a job that ends in a whole-Extra write-back. Writers that write Extra on the same item inside the window from the same add: Linter's add lint after at least 500 ms (row 9), citation-tally on add (row 20), Zoplicate's add-time merge at keep or discard (row 2), and the opt-in add paths of rows 21, 23, 45 and 51. Writers that can overlap any job, a menu-run one included: zotero-openalex (row 17), open-citations with writeExtra on (row 22), Cita in extra mode (row 16), FLoRA's monthly pass (row 54), Zotero MCP with writes on (row 44), the Extra-writing commands of rows 8, 9, 11, 12, 15 and 16, and the person. BBT and DOI Manager write no Extra there. research-vault writes none: capture sends its item records whole in one create call, so their Extra exists before the add notifier fires. Verifier: upheld; the re-judgement's capture cite should read research_vault/capture.py:602-676 (the file has 676 lines at b06e212), and trash_item, the client's other item write, has callers only in tests (lib.js:34-42, 150-168, 217-246, 274-373 @ v1.0.3 asset sha256 01b30c1a…, no licence, paraphrased; research_vault/capture.py:629-649; research_vault/zotero.py:390-423 @ b06e212).
47. **The PMCID fetcher fetches tags for a PMCID-only item by the wrong id** (row 5; verifier upheld and reproduced it). When an item reaches the tag fetch with a PMCID and no PMID, the efetch id is the PMCID, which PubMed reads as the PMID with the same digits, so an unrelated article's MeSH headings and keywords become tags. Live reads on 2026-10-04: efetch from PubMed with id PMC4408209 returned PMID 4408209, a 1974 paper on prostaglandins and ovulation, while NCBI's idconv maps PMC4408209 to PMID 25461789. Keyed by a real PMID the tag fetch is by identifier: the PMID comes from the item's own field or Extra line, from esearch on the DOI (an exact-phrase search of the Publisher ID field that must return exactly one hit, insensitive only to punctuation and case), or from idconv by DOI with an exact DOI match. extensions.zotero.pmcid.tags=false removes the tag fetch from both triggers. Endpoint drift, deciding no cell, also upheld: the fetcher's idconv URL answers 301 to pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov and works only because fetch follows redirects; its email parameter value itself begins with the parameter name; and its Extra-field pattern matches a literal s where optional whitespace was meant (lib.js:108, 274-302, 304-340, 341-363 @ v1.0.3 asset; NCBI efetch, esearch and idconv reads, 2026-10-04).
48. **Attachment Scanner's PubMed-entry test is a title match** (row 26; verifier upheld). With remove_pubmed_entry on, it trashes a non-file attachment whose display title is exactly PubMed entry; it never reads the link URL, the parent's PMID field or its Extra, and never compares an id. Schema 44 gives a native PMID field to journalArticle only, and Zotero's PubMed XML translator writes the id as a PMID Extra line on books and book sections while making the same link. So the scanner can trash a link whose PMID the item does not otherwise carry: where the PMID was later removed or changed, where a merge moved a PubMed entry under a master without it, on any type other than journalArticle, and on any link someone titled PubMed entry. Production, read-only GETs on 2026-10-04: 8 PubMed entries are in the trash, all linked_url under live journalArticle parents whose PMID field holds the link's id, so each did duplicate a kept PMID; which writer trashed them is not proven. None of 1,431 live attachments is titled PubMed entry (attachmentscanner.js:445-457 @ v0.5.1 xpi sha256 37b5da64…, MIT; zotero-schema schema.json:1583, 1646 @ 70c3aa9, paraphrased; translators PubMed XML.js:258-268, 353-363 @ dddcf49).
49. **research-vault's re-key detection, on which part (3)'s exception relies, has two gaps and a precondition** (identity-hygiene re-judgement; verifier upheld, with an addition). Recorded as research-vault limitations; no cell moves on them. First, propagate refuses to rename onto an existing literature note, so where a command gives one item another item's key (arXiv Workflow's merge, when both items have notes) the re-key is detected but not repaired. Second, lifecycle classifies a note trashed before re-keyed when any tuple attachment is in the trash, so a command that re-keys and trashes a recorded PDF in one go (arXiv Workflow with trashUnannotatedPDF) masks the re-key from propagate (items 37 and 41). Verifier addition: the re-keyed branch needs a non-empty live key, so a path that blanks an existing key (an arXiv Workflow merge from an unkeyed published item, Zutilo's pasteJSONAll from a keyless source) is not classified re-keyed until BBT fills a new key, and never without BBT or in a read-only library. Row 4's pass rests on none of these, because its settings remove the merge (research_vault/propagate.py:184-194; research_vault/lifecycle.py:88-94 @ b06e212).
50. **Zotero 10.0.5's merge pane does not fill empty fields** (row 2; verifier upheld). The pane overlays only the JSON of the master clone it showed, where the person picked alternatives, excluding relations, collections and tags, then calls mergeItems.mjs. Zoplicate's merge() spreads the losers' JSON under the master's before that call, so the losers fill the master's empty fields; that fill is Zoplicate's own write, outside #183 (Zotero chrome/content/zotero/elements/duplicatesMergePane.js:166-211 @ ca61760, paraphrased; src/shared/duplicates/mergeItems.ts:16-29 @ 1d95191).
51. **How Zoplicate's \_saveData patch picks a new parent** (row 2; verifier upheld, saying the heuristic was understated). When the trashed parent was not merged there is no dc:replaces successor, the candidate list is empty and the search spans the whole library, restricted only to the trashed parent's item type: the trashed parent's DOI as a substring of any DOI, url or extra value, then title, then one matching creator, then year within one. Each step returns as soon as one candidate remains, so a single DOI-substring hit or a single title hit becomes the parent with no creator or year check, and a DOI that is a prefix of another, or one cited in another item's Extra, can pick a different work. The patch is always on, applies to every writer, and no preference gates it (src/db/duplicateFinder.ts:18-33, 74-110, 147-179, 257-264; src/integrations/zotero/patches/patchItemSaveData.ts:24-36 @ 1d95191).
52. **What Zotero's recognizer writes that is not an identifier lookup** (rows 29, 46, 50 and 51; verifier upheld, with a hedge). With no identifier, or when translating the identifier it found fails, it builds the parent from the recognizer service's response: title, creators, year, pages, volume, container and publisher. On its arXiv, DOI and ISBN branches it fills abstract and language from the service response wherever the looked-up item lacks them; the client code shows that the client did not look them up, not how the service obtained them. recognizeItems has no preference gate; only autoRecognizeItems checks extensions.zotero.autoRecognizeFiles, so an add-on that calls recognizeItems, or ZoteroPane.recognizeSelected, writes these values whatever that preference says (Zotero chrome/content/zotero/xpcom/recognizeDocument.js:112-125, 141-143, 432-574; chrome/content/zotero/zoteroPane.js:6296-6297 @ ca61760).
53. **A path that does not resolve is not a definite invalidity test** (rows 26, 31 and 46; verifier upheld). getFilePathAsync returns false for a linked URL, an empty path, an invalid stored path, a .zotero file, a stored file absent from the local storage directory (one not yet downloaded by file sync included), and a base-relative linked path with no base directory set; fileExists() is its boolean. Attachment Scanner also treats any exception as broken. These tests establish only that the file does not resolve here now, not that the attachment is invalid in a synced library. Duplicate Cleaner's resource test treats a pending download as unknown, and its post-merge PDF test does not (Zotero chrome/content/zotero/xpcom/data/item.js:2899-3070, 3183-3197 @ ca61760; attachmentscanner.js:411-428 @ v0.5.1; zotadata.js:3842-3865 @ v1.5.5; resource-cleanup.js:12-28 and bootstrap.js:17-27 @ 265f303).
54. **Under the third ruling nothing separates the toolkit debug bridge from row 42's execJS** (identity-hygiene re-judgement; verifier upheld, with two corrections). zotero://ztoolkit-debug, registered on the shared toolkit global by the bundles of rows 2 and 4 among the other toolkit bundlers (item 36), runs caller-supplied JavaScript after a window confirm, or with no confirm when the caller matches extensions.zotero.debug-bridge.password. The confirm was what item 36 and §2a's toolkit-route bullet rested on, and it no longer counts. Whether an arbitrary-code route is a (1), (2) or (3) path is item 38, still unruled: row 4's pass turns on it, row 2 fails regardless, and if item 38 is resolved against such routes, every toolkit bundler fails with no add-on setting that confines the bridge. Verifier corrections: a third no-prompt path exists, because the bridge skips both password and confirm when a bypass flag on the shared toolkit global is true, a flag any bundler can set (the remote-write-routes check found none setting it); and the password preference does not remove the route but replaces the confirm with a shared-secret match, the secret sitting in prefs.js, so no preference removes it (item 39 for host preferences) (v5.1.1 asset chrome/content/scripts/zoplicate.js:336-407, 497-504; v0.3.9 asset content/scripts/arxiv-workflow.js:214-285, 375-382).
55. **Readings the third ruling leaves open** (all families; the answers change §3 results). They are listed under §2a "Third ruling", each with the cells that turn on it: rows 3, 4, 5, 12, 14, 20, 24, 25, 26, 27, 30, 32, 36, 38 and 50, and rows 37 and 53, where the verifier refuted the re-judgement's reading.
